# Design Document

## Overview

This design makes RF-DETR-Seg (rfdetr 1.10.1, `RFDETRSegNano` … `RFDETRSeg2XLarge`) a third detector family in the pipeline that `rfdetr-training-and-transfer-learning` and `detector-checkpoint-import` built, and gives the device a second result shape. Every stage reuses the plumbing those two specs left behind: the arch-keyed constant maps of `detection_training.py` (`DETECTION_ARCHES`, `ENTRY_POINT_FOR_ARCH`, … at `edge-cv-portal/backend/layers/shared/python/detection_training.py:42-146`) gain one `rf_detr_seg` key each; the SageMaker launch, record shape, compile skip and `package_trained_detection_component` are reused; the `.pth` conversion job, probe and validators gain a Seg branch beside the detection one; Smart Import's existing three-output path is hardened rather than replaced. What is new in kind is (a) an instance-segmentation labeling modality and manifest (D3), (b) a device decoder with two Output_Modes (D1) and (c) an additive extension of the `task = object_detection` Detection_List so each entry carries its mask as Canonical_RLE (O1 a, O2 b), which flows through the device DB, run preview, HMI, Portal `ResultsViewer` and the inference-results export. Because the decoder, the Triton templates, the device DB readers and the HMI live in the LocalServer image, every one of the six LocalServer components is rebuilt and a per-architecture feature floor keeps a Seg workflow off older builds (D5, O11 a). All six Seg sizes are offered and DDA's PML list is corrected to the package's licence metadata (D4). The published JP6 component is replaced by a fresh import of the same ONNX through the hardened Smart Import (D5; §14).

Owner decisions D1–D5 and the recorded answers to O1–O16 (`owner-decisions.md`, 2026-11-18) are binding; this document numbers its own choices **DD1–DD20** so they are never confused with the owner's D1–D5. Code facts cite `path:line` at 43cd4ff1; vendor facts cite rfdetr 1.10.1 through `web-answers.md` (WQ-req-author-1/2/3).

```
Labeling (InstanceSegmentation) ──▶ Instance_Manifest (one line/image, per-instance Canonical_RLE)
        │                                        │ manifest_to_detector_dataset.py --format coco --coco-layout rfdetr
        ▼                                        ▼ (COCO `segmentation` = uncompressed RLE dict)
CreateTraining.tsx ── POST /training {model_type: instance_segmentation, detection_arch: rf_detr_seg,
        │                            output_mode, base_model, hyperparameters: {rfdetr_seg_size, mask_threshold, …}}
        ▼
training.py::_create_detection_training_job  (Seg_Switch 403 first; parser/env/metrics/record per arch map)
        │  SageMaker script-mode: train_rfdetr_seg.py (+ train_rfdetr.py, _common.py, converter) on the PyTorch DLC
        │  → RFDETRSeg<Size>(pretrain_weights, num_classes).train() → export_onnx → cap IR ≤ 10
        │  → verify_seg_outputs: [1,Q,4] + [1,Q,C+1] + [1,Q,R/4,R/4] → training_metadata.json (3 shapes, names, hints)
        ▼
POST /training/{id}/package → package_trained_detection_component: build_detection_device_manifest(rf_detr_seg, output_mode)
        │  stage dir = stage_type_for('rf_detr_seg', output_mode) ∈ {rf_detr_instance_segmentation | rf_detr_semantic_segmentation}
        ▼
Device (LocalServer ≥ Seg_Feature_Floor[arch]):
  OnnxRunner → RfDetrSegPostProcessor (mode ⇐ stage type)
     instance: [InstanceSegmentationResult(box, class, score, packed mask)]  → base model: Detection_List + mask_rle/mask_area,
               Composite_Mask painted → palette `mask` tensor → marshal: {"detections", "mask_color_map", detections_truncated},
               <id>.mask.png, overlay.jpg (boxes + fills), <id>.detections.json
     semantic: AnomalyResult(score, Composite_Mask)                         → today's anomaly-shaped record, unchanged
  device DB `detections` column (list form) · run preview `/overlay` {maskImage, maskBackground, instances} · HMI mask canvas
Portal: captures.py (page: has_masks, mask_area, detections_truncated, jsonl_key, never mask_rle; detail: ?key=<jsonl_key>&include_masks=1 → one capture with mask_rle, < 6 MB by DD4) → ResultsTab fetches the detail for the selected capture → ResultsViewer (masks+boxes | boxes | server overlay)
Smart Import: .pth → Conversion_Job (task gate, 3-output contract, mask-IoU parity) · ONNX → inspect (names, Q, grid) → shared builder
```

The two precedent designs' structure is followed: decisions, per-component architecture, data contracts, interfaces, error handling, testing strategy, and the spike's open questions. Requirement ids are cited inline as **Req n.m**.

## Decisions

**DD1 — The Seg entry point is its own file, `train_rfdetr_seg.py`, importing the pure helpers of `train_rfdetr.py` as a sibling module.** Req 4.1 leaves "own file or mode" to the design. A mode flag inside `train_rfdetr.py` would make `ENTRY_POINT_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg']` equal to the detection entry point and the task selection depend on an environment variable, and it would grow the file whose constants `test_train_rfdetr_static.py:91-100` pins. A separate file keeps `SIZE_CLASSES`, `RESOLUTION_STEP` and `build_metadata` untouched and lets the Seg table live apart (Req 4.1). `train_rfdetr.py` imports only stdlib and `_common` at module level (`train_rfdetr.py:62-69`; rfdetr/torch/onnx are lazy), so `import train_rfdetr as det` is safe inside the Seg script. Cost: the Seg sourcedir must carry `train_rfdetr.py` as an extra sibling, which `build_sourcedir_tarball` (`detection_training.py:654-702`) does not know about today. That function has **no arch parameter**: it is keyed by entry point through `_REQUIREMENTS_FOR_ENTRY_POINT = {ENTRY_POINT_FOR_ARCH[a]: REQUIREMENTS_FOR_ARCH[a] for a in DETECTION_ARCHES}` (`:649-651`, looked up at `:678`). So a new arch-keyed map `SOURCEDIR_SIBLINGS_FOR_ARCH = {'yolo': (), 'rf_detr': (), 'rf_detr_seg': ('train_rfdetr.py',)}` extends the map pattern of Req 1.1, and its entry-point mirror `_SIBLINGS_FOR_ENTRY_POINT = {ENTRY_POINT_FOR_ARCH[a]: SOURCEDIR_SIBLINGS_FOR_ARCH[a] for a in DETECTION_ARCHES}` is consulted beside `:678`; the signature stays `(code_dir, out_path, entry_point=DETECTION_ENTRY_POINT)`. Two existing tests react differently: the arch-parametrised `test_job_environment_base_weights_only_when_passed` (`test_detection_training_shared.py:612`) needs no edit, but `test_build_sourcedir_tarball_tolerates_missing_common` (`:718-726`) is parametrised over `sorted(dt.ENTRY_POINT_FOR_ARCH.values())` and asserts the **exact** member set `{entry_point, 'requirements.txt', 'manifest_to_detector_dataset.py', 'dedupe_frames.py'}`, so with `train_rfdetr_seg.py` in the map it fails twice: the fixture `_CODE_DIR_FILES` (`:647-655`) lacks the file (`build_sourcedir_tarball` raises `FileNotFoundError`, as `:333-340` pins), and once the fixture has it the Seg tarball carries the sibling `train_rfdetr.py` (five members). That pin moves with the change (fixture gains `'train_rfdetr_seg.py': '# rf-detr-seg entry point\n'`; expected set = the four fixed members ∪ `set(dt._SIBLINGS_FOR_ENTRY_POINT[entry_point])`), extended, never relaxed; it is the second named exception of Requirement 17.1 (correction C12). `build_sourcedir.sh --arch rf_detr_seg`, the `TrainingHandler` asset list (`compute-stack.ts:1491-1499`) and the Export_Image (`detector-export-image/Dockerfile:64`, `build-and-push.sh:83`) copy the same file (§4, §14).

**DD2 — One decoder class, `RfDetrSegPostProcessor`, in a new module `rf_detr_seg_postprocessor.py`; the mode is the stage type; the old module becomes an import shim.** O16 a fixes two stage types mapped to one class (Req 9.1). The class reads `config['type']` (the stage dict `ModelGraphFactory` constructs it with carries the stage's `type`, `model_graph_factory.py:69, 78-79`): `rf_detr_instance_segmentation` → instance, anything else (including the D5 manifest, whose stage type is `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation`, and the eight existing tests, which pass a config without `type`, `test_rf_detr_semantic_segmentation_postprocessor.py:46-53`) → semantic. `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation_postprocessor.py` keeps existing as `from .rf_detr_seg_postprocessor import RfDetrSegPostProcessor as RfDetrSemanticSegmentationPostProcessor` plus the three `DEFAULT_*` names, so the eight tests import unchanged (Req 9.7) and nothing in the registry breaks. `MODEL_NAME_MAP` (`model_processors/__init__.py:52-65`) keeps `"financier": ("rf_detr_semantic_segmentation", RfDetrSegPostProcessor)` and adds `"macaron": ("rf_detr_instance_segmentation", RfDetrSegPostProcessor)`. Rejected: renaming the existing module (forces edits to the eight tests beyond O3 b's edge-pixel adjustments) and two classes (duplicates the decode).

**DD3 — Dense masks never outlive the decode loop: each kept mask is stored bit-packed (`numpy.packbits`, row-major) on the result, the Composite_Mask is painted from the packed form, and the RLE is produced at serialisation.** Req 10.3 asks the design to bound in-process mask memory between decode and serialisation. The decoder (Req 9.4) upsamples one kept query's logits at a time to the source frame, thresholds, derives `mask_area` and the tight box check, packs the boolean mask (`np.packbits(mask.ravel())`, 1 bit/pixel: 259,200 bytes at 1920×1080 against 2,073,600 for `bool`) and drops the dense array before the next query. Worst case at `top_k = 300` and 1920×1080 is 300 × 259 KB ≈ 78 MB, against ≈ 622 MB dense; typical runs (tens of instances) stay under 10 MB. Unpacking (`np.unpackbits(bits, count=h*w).reshape(h, w)`) is a vectorised copy, so painting the composite in the Triton base model (Req 10.1) unpacks one instance at a time with one dense buffer alive. The RLE string is produced in `InstanceSegmentationResult.serialize()` by the vectorised encoder of DD5, once per entry, where the Detection_List is built. Latency cost per instance: one packbits + one unpackbits (each a few ms for 2 MP on Jetson-class CPUs) plus the RLE pass (one `np.diff` over the column-major flattening); the spike (task 0) measures and records the numbers in `docs/multi-runtime-inference.md` §24 (Req 16.1). Rejected: an instance cap below `top_k` (loses detections the model made and still leaves ~2 MB per kept instance) and RLE-at-decode (painting would need an RLE decode per instance, slower than unpackbits).

**DD4 — The Detection_List byte ceiling is 4 MiB of serialised JSON, enforced once, in the Triton base model, with a per-entry marker.** The `anomalies` tensor is a variable-length `uint8` vector (`dda_triton/model_convertor.py:659-661`), so the base model is the single point that produces the list every downstream copy re-serialises (`marshal_for_capture_template.py:437-492`, the record `.jsonl`, the sidecar, `save_image_object`'s dict). `__build_detection_tensors` serialises all entries, and while `len(json.dumps(payload)) > DETECTION_LIST_MAX_BYTES` it removes `mask_rle` from the lowest-confidence entry that still has one and sets `mask_truncated: true` on it (boxes and `mask_area` stay). The marshal derives `detections_truncated = any(entry.get('mask_truncated'))` and writes it beside the `detections` map in the label block and the sidecar (Req 10.3, the one marker name); `captures.py` passes it through and the viewer lists the entries with `mask_area > 0` and no `mask_rle` (Req 11.2). 4 MiB fits ~80 complex 1080p masks (≈ 50 KB each as text) and several hundred typical ones, while keeping the base64 copy in the `.jsonl` line (~5.6 MB) and the live-result WebSocket frame within what the HMI already handles for `maskImage` (a base64 PNG of the frame). The Composite_Mask PNG is outside the ceiling. **The same ceiling bounds the one Portal response that carries the RLE:** the per-capture detail request `GET /captures?key=<jsonl_key>&include_masks=1` (§11) re-serialises exactly one Detection_List through `_normalize_detections` (seven keys per entry, none longer than the device's; `json.dumps` with default separators on both sides, `marshal_for_capture_template.py:485`, `auth_middleware.py:271`) plus a few hundred bytes of envelope, so its body is ≈ 4.2 MB at most — under the AWS Lambda synchronous response cap of 6 MB (`web-answers.md` WQ-design-review-1: "Invocation payload (request and response): 6 MB each … (synchronous)", not increasable; API Gateway REST payload 10 MB, not increasable). `GET /captures` is a buffered `LambdaIntegration` (`api-gateway-stack.ts:199, 211, 404-410`) returning an uncompressed JSON body (`auth_middleware.py:248-271`), so the Lambda cap applies first. Consequence, stated once here and pinned by the 4 MiB fixture in `tests/test_captures_instances.py` (`len(body) < 6_000_000`): `DETECTION_LIST_MAX_BYTES` may not be raised above 4 MiB without redesigning that path (a presigned sidecar URL would be the alternative, §11 "Rejected").

**DD5 — One Canonical_RLE codec on the device, vectorised, pinned byte-for-byte to `dda_manifest.rle_encode` by a shared vector file.** `rle_encode` (`dda_manifest.py:170-204`) is a pure-Python loop over `width × height` pixels — too slow for 2 MP masks on a Jetson. A new pure module `src/backend/lyra_science_processing_utils/utils/mask_rle.py` exposes exactly two functions, `encode(mask) -> str` and `decode(rle, width, height) -> np.ndarray[bool]` (these two names are used in every section of this document). `encode` implements the same encoding with numpy: `flat = mask.T.reshape(-1)` (column-major order), `changes = np.flatnonzero(flat[1:] != flat[:-1]) + 1`, `counts = np.diff(np.concatenate(([0], changes, [flat.size])))`, a leading `0` when `flat[0]` is foreground, `' '.join(map(str, counts))`. Its inverse `decode(rle, width, height)` (`np.repeat`) serves the composite painter's tests and the export path. Byte identity is pinned by `test/fixtures/canonical_rle_vectors.json` — masks (border-touching, hollow, empty, full, single pixel) with the counts string produced by `dda_manifest.rle_encode` — read by `test/backend-test/test_mask_rle.py` and by `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/test_dda_manifest_instance.py` (Req 10.2). Rejected: importing `dda_manifest` on the device (the shared layer is a Portal Lambda layer, not in the LocalServer image).

**DD6 — The Seg branch of `generate_dda_package` calls the shared builder instead of copying it.** The detection precedent kept `build_detection_device_manifest` as a copy of `generate_dda_package`'s branch and pinned equality with `test_detection_manifest_parity.py` because the shared layer must not import the handler module (`detection_training.py:560-567`). The reverse import is already routine — `model_converter.py` imports `detector_conversion as dconv` and `classify_checkpoint` from the layer, and `ModelConverterHandler` has `sharedLayer` attached (`compute-stack.ts:1799-1813`) — so the new `is_rf_detr_seg` branch calls `detection_training.build_detection_device_manifest(..., detection_arch='rf_detr_seg', output_mode=…)` and pops the `dataset` block (which `package_onnx_component` merges later from `config.yaml`, as for every import). Parity becomes structural; `test_detection_manifest_parity.py` is still extended with Seg cases (Req 6.2) because it also guards the `dataset` merge and the key order the device reads. The detection branches (`model_converter.py:1017-1035`) stay byte-identical.

**DD7 — Seg_Switch mechanics (O12 b): context key `enableRfDetrSeg` → Lambda variable `PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED` on exactly three Lambdas → frontend field `rfDetrSegEnabled` with absent = ON.** `context-helpers.ts` gains `rfDetrSegEnabled(ctx): boolean` = the `groundedSamWorkerEnabled` rule (`:11-20`, only an explicit false disables). `compute-stack.ts` builds `const rfDetrSegEnvironment = { PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED: rfDetrSegEnabled(this.node.tryGetContext('enableRfDetrSeg')) ? 'true' : 'false' }` and spreads it into `TrainingHandler`, `ModelConverterHandler` and `PackagingHandler` only (Req 15.1; never `lambdaEnvironment`, which the exact-key oracle `grounded-sam-worker-infra.test.ts:248-318` pins). The backend reads it through one helper in the shared layer, `detection_training.rfdetr_seg_enabled() -> bool` (`os.environ.get('PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED', 'true').strip().lower() != 'false'`), so an absent variable is ON, the same default the CDK applies. The frontend mirror is the `registryEnforced` pattern (`frontend/src/config.ts:22-33`) with the default inverted: `config.ts` gains `rfDetrSegEnabled?: boolean` and a helper `isRfDetrSegEnabled(config) = config.rfDetrSegEnabled !== false`; a new `CfnOutput` `RfDetrSegEnabled` ('true' | 'false') on `EdgeCVPortalComputeStack` is read by `deploy-frontend.sh` beside `AssistantApiUrl` (`deploy-frontend.sh:89-128`) and written as a boolean into `config.json`; when the output is absent (an older stack) the field is removed, which the frontend reads as ON. This resolves requirements-review pass 5 NIT 3 (the `assistantApiUrl` pattern hides when absent). For the ONNX inspect response when OFF (NIT 4), `inspect_model_endpoint` keeps `suggested_type = 'instance_segmentation'` and adds `disabled_reason: "RF-DETR-Seg is disabled on this portal"` plus the same text in `architecture_hints`. For a `.pth` the mechanism is the existing one: `inspect_checkpoint_file` (`model_converter.py:253-266`) already calls `dconv.assess_checkpoint(probe, conversion_available=unavailable is None, unavailable_reason=unavailable)` (`:262-263`); when `probe['evidence'].get('rfdetr_task') == 'segmentation'` and the switch is OFF it passes `conversion_available=False, unavailable_reason='RF-DETR-Seg is disabled on this portal'`, so the `checkpoint` block carries `convertible: false` with that reason — `conversionLocksFor` then returns `null` and the form keeps PyTorch/Neo (`SmartImport.tsx:261-263`), exactly the "not convertible" behaviour of today (Req 15.2).

**DD8 — The Seg feature floor is a third floor beside the lineage and stream floors, gated in the packager after model records are resolved and in the deployment gate from the `has_rfdetr_seg_model` discriminator.** The stream gate runs before compilation from node types (`workflow_packaging.py:2638-2645`); the Seg discriminator depends on model *records*, which the packager loads only after compilation in `resolve_model_components` (`:2765-2768`, via `build_model_registry_snapshot`, `:1734`). To read the registry once, `resolve_model_components` gains an optional `records: Dict[str, dict]` argument and a sibling `load_model_records(model_names, usecase_id)` returns the snapshot's records (the first 20 lines of today's function, same `PackagingError` for a missing record); the handler calls `load_model_records`, computes `seg_models = rfdetr_seg_model_names(records)` (records whose `detection.detection_arch == 'rf_detr_seg'`), runs `rfdetr_seg_arch_gate_findings(seg_models, architectures)` → 409 `RFDETR_SEG_UNSUPPORTED_ARCH` with findings when an architecture has no entry, then passes `rfdetr_seg=has_rfdetr_seg` (computed once as `bool(seg_models)` after the gate) into `local_server_component_dependencies` (`:2772-2773`, forwarded to `min_local_server_version_for` at `:2029`) and into `build_manifest(..., rfdetr_seg=has_rfdetr_seg)` (`:2829-2837`), which threads it to `min_local_server_version_for` (`:2200-2201`) and `min_local_server_versions_map` (`:2208-2209`) — the stream precedent's own shape (`build_manifest` docstring `:2179-2184`, comment `:2204-2207`); the handler calls neither floor helper itself, and `build_manifest` is the **only** caller of `min_local_server_versions_map` in the repository, so without this widening a Seg workflow would carry the lineage floors only and the device (`src/backend/workflow_engine/discovery.py:212-240`, `minLocalServerVersions[arch]` else the scalar → `STATUS_INVALID`) would never see the Seg floor (C23); the new keyword defaults to `False` everywhere (resolving `max(lineage, stream?, seg?)`) so a workflow without a Seg model stamps and resolves byte-identically. At the pinned base (`aa6d912f`, base-drift BD1) `build_manifest` and `build_arch_zip` already end in a keyword-only `*, reference_files: Optional[Sequence[PackagedReference]] = None` (`workflow_packaging.py:2245-2246`), so the widening is `build_manifest(…, stream_features: bool = False, rfdetr_seg: bool = False, *, reference_files=None)` and the handler's call to extend is `:2937-2947` (base lines; 43cd4ff1 `:2829-2837`). It then writes `has_rfdetr_seg_model = :hrs` unconditionally in the version-item update (`:2931-2947`). Compilation having run before the 409 is harmless: it is pure and no version item is written before the update. `deployments.py` mirrors the stream path: `WORKFLOW_RFDETR_SEG_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS = _parse_min_versions_map(...)`, `rfdetr_seg_feature_floor_for(arch)`, `check_local_server_compatibility(..., rfdetr_seg=False)` rejecting a device whose arch has no entry (reason names the architecture and the required build) and the 409 details gain `rfdetr_seg_model: True` and `rfdetr_seg_min_local_server_versions` (Req 14.8 b, c). The alias record of Req 8.5 carries `detection_arch = 'rf_detr_seg'`, so it is gated; the untouched D5 record (`segmentation` + `rf_detr`) is not, which is today's behaviour.

**DD9 — Instance annotations store RLE only; the polygon is client state; the server derives the box.** O7 a and Req 2.2 fix the stored geometry as Canonical_RLE. The canvas rasterises the closed polygon (even-odd scanline fill into the label bitmap it already keeps for Segmentation, `edge-cv-portal/frontend/src/components/labeling/AnnotationCanvas.tsx:404-414`) and encodes it with the existing `encodeRleColumnMajor` (`:102`); the polygon vertices stay in component state for editing and are discarded on save. `dda_labeling._validate_annotation` (`:2622`) gains the `InstanceSegmentation` branch: it validates each `rle` and **recomputes the tight box** from it with a new **pure-Python** helper `dda_manifest.tight_box_from_rle(rle, width, height)` (§2; no `numpy` — the labeling Lambdas carry `[sharedLayer, imagingLayer]` only, `compute-stack.ts:2856, 2905`, and `layers/imaging/requirements.txt` is `Pillow==12.3.0` alone; `numpy==1.26.4` lives only in `layers/video/requirements.txt`, which they do not attach), rejects an empty mask naming the instance, and overwrites whatever box the client sent — the server is the owner of the "derived, never typed" invariant (Req 2.2) because the client can be stale or hostile. Pre-labels from `sam`/`grounded-sam`/`llm` arrive as Segmentation-shaped `regions` (`dda_labeling.py:6379-6417`; the worker contract is unchanged, Req 2.4) and are mapped one region → one instance (class carried, `null` kept) by the task-fetch code, with the box derived the same way. Rejected: storing polygons (a second geometry beside RLE for brush/SAM/device masks; O8 a) and client-authoritative boxes.

**DD10 — The COCO writer emits `segmentation` as an uncompressed RLE dict copied one-to-one from the manifest; `--format yolo` refuses an instance manifest.** WQ-req-author-1 settled that rfdetr 1.10.1 decodes `{"counts": [ints], "size": [h, w]}` through `pycocotools.frPyObjects` using the dict's own `size` (`coco.py:396-464`), so the converter copies the Canonical_RLE counts (parsed from the space-separated string to ints) and writes `size = [image_height, image_width]`, asserting `sum(counts) == h × w` and dropping (with a warning) an instance that fails it (Req 3.3). `area` is the foreground pixel count (sum of the odd-indexed runs), never the box area. The YOLO layout has no mask representation DDA trains with, so `--format yolo` on an instance manifest exits with an error naming the format (no silent box-only dataset). Negatives, grouping and splits are the bbox path's (Req 3.4): `parse_entries` produces the same record shape with an added `instances` list, so `group_by_similarity`, `split_groups` and `place_image` run unchanged.

**DD11 — Detection_List entries gain an explicit integer `class_index`; `class` keeps the detection decoder's meaning.** `RfDetrDetectionPostProcessor._make_result` sets `obj_class` to the class **label** when `detection.class_names` is present, else the index string (`rf_detr_detection_postprocessor.py:200-219`), and the marshal copies `class` into `class_index` (`marshal_for_capture_template.py:453`). The composite painter needs the numeric class (palette row `class + 1`, Req 9.5, 10.1), so `InstanceSegmentationResult` carries `class_index: int` and serialises it; `__build_detection_tensors` keeps `class`/`class_label` exactly as for detection, and the marshal prefers `det['class_index']` when present (`str(det.get('class_index', det.get('class', '')))`). Entries without the key — every YOLO/RF-DETR detection entry — produce today's record byte-for-byte (Req 10.1, 17.1).

**DD12 — The instance-mode colour map is computed by the marshal from the palette mask it already holds, through one pure helper.** Req 10.1 fixes the rule (background share from palette row 0; one entry per class present with the share of pixels equal to that class's palette row; labels from `class_label`; `.tolist()` before `hex_color_string`; never `convert_color_mask_to_index_mask`). The helper `instance_color_map(mask_rgb, entries, palette) -> Dict[str, Dict]` lives in the marshal template (the marshal must stay import-light, `marshal_for_capture_template.py:36-61`), returns `{"0": {...}, "1": {...}}` with the label key chosen by the caller (`name` for `internal-color-map`, `class-name` for `mask_color_map`), and is unit-tested with synthetic masks. The `try/except ImportError` guard around `DEFAULT_ANOMALY_MASK_PALETTE`/`hex_color_string` degrades to "mask without colour map", as Req 10.1 specifies.

**DD13 — Device DB: a nullable `detections` JSON column in list form, Alembic revision chained to the current head `37b6e36641e7`.** Req 10.5b names the four sites (ORM, marshmallow schema + plain class, pydantic history model, single-row read). The migration's `down_revision` is `'37b6e36641e7'` (`alembic/metadata_database/versions/37b6e36641e7_add_capture_type.py:33-34` is the head at 43cd4ff1; `ddb37f5617fc_adding_modelthresholds_to_inference_result.py` is the *pattern* file the requirement names, not the head). `op.add_column('inference_result_metadata', sa.Column('detections', sa.JSON(), nullable=True))`, downgrade drops it. The list form is the map's entries in ascending integer key order (`convert_inference_res_to_save_in_db`), so `shaping.resolve_path` can fan `results[].detections[].mask_rle` (edge MCP, Req 13.3).

**DD14 — The run preview endpoint gains `instances`; one HMI helper normalises the three sources.** `GET /workflows/executions/{id}/overlay` (`workflow_engine/api.py:481-499`) returns `read_mask_overlay(...)`, which becomes `{maskImage, maskBackground, instances, detectionsTruncated}`; `instances` is the list form with `mask_rle` read from the sidecar first, then the capture record (`detections.read_detections`, `:118-151`), or `null`. The HMI module `src/frontend/src/components/deployed-workflow/instanceDetections.ts` (beside the existing `detections.ts`; `src/frontend/src` has no `utils/` directory at 43cd4ff1 and HMI helpers sit next to the components that use them) exports `normalizeDetectionEntries(mapOrList)`, `decodeMaskRle(rle, w, h): Uint8Array` and an `InstanceMaskLayer` canvas component used by RunResults (preview endpoint), LiveResultCard (inference-result dict, map form) and the result-history detail (DB row, list form) (Req 10.6). HMI tests are Jest (`src/frontend/package.json` `"test": "react-scripts test"`), not vitest; only `edge-cv-portal/frontend` is vitest.

**DD15 — `convert_model` inspects the ONNX graph itself for `instance_segmentation` and `segmentation` requests.** Today the ONNX import never looks at the graph in `convert_model` (`model_converter.py:1226-1227`: a stub `model_info`); the inspection happens only in the separate `/models/inspect` call the UI makes. The Seg contract (Req 8.1), the alias and the 400 of Req 8.5 need the output shapes and names at convert time, so `convert_model` calls the existing pure `inspect_onnx_model(local_model)` (protobuf parse, `:600-694`) for those two model types and derives `num_queries`, the mask grid and `output_names` from it. Other model types keep the stub (byte-identical, Req 8.8).

**DD16 — Output_Mode is a record field and a manifest field; the stage type is resolved by `stage_type_for(arch, output_mode)`; re-packaging switches modes.** O15 a / O16 a. `detection.output_mode` is written by training, conversion and import; `stage_type_for('rf_detr_seg', 'instance')` → `rf_detr_instance_segmentation`, `('rf_detr_seg', 'semantic')` → `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation`, any other arch → `STAGE_TYPE_FOR_ARCH[arch]`. The Package action accepts `output_mode` for Seg records (Req 6.4), persists `packaged_output_mode` on the record beside `packaged_components` (`packaging.py:1102`), and the device reads only the manifest (Req 9.6). The override applies to the records `package_trained_detection_component` builds (trained Seg records and Seg Conversion_Records); for an imported ONNX Seg record — routed by `is_onnx_import` (`packaging.py:203-221`) to `package_onnx_component` (`:1313-1327`), which Requirement 8.6 leaves unchanged and which re-zips the import-time package without rebuilding its manifest — a present `output_mode` → 400 `output_mode cannot be changed for an imported ONNX model: its manifest was fixed at import; re-import with the other output_mode`, an absent one packages as today and writes no `packaged_output_mode`; the frontend offers the select only for those records and shows 'Output mode is fixed at import; re-import to change it' for an imported ONNX Seg record (C34).

**DD17 — Conversion parity for Seg adds a mask-IoU term on the native `R/4` grid; the reference returns three tensors.** `convert_rfdetr` is generalised to `convert_rfdetr(checkpoint, cfg)` with `cfg.task`; after `reference_module.export()` (`export_checkpoint.py:906-907`) rfdetr 1.10.1's `LWDETR.forward_export` returns a **tuple**, never a dict — `(pred_boxes, pred_logits, pred_masks)` for a Seg model (`lwdetr.py:623-717`, `:712-713`; `web-answers.md` WQ-design-author-3), with tuple position i ↔ ONNX output i (`detr.py:1800-1808`) and `pred_masks` the dense `(1, Q, R/4, R/4)` logits — so the closure (`:909-914`) returns `[t.detach().cpu().numpy() for t in out[:3]]` when `cfg.task == 'segmentation'` and `out[:2]` otherwise; the `isinstance(out, dict)` branch at `:912-913` is kept for detection and is dead for Seg (the `pred_masks` dict key exists only in the training forward, `lwdetr.py:568`). `compare_rfdetr_parity` computes, for every matched pair above `score_floor`, the IoU of `sigmoid(mask) > mask_threshold` between reference and export at the native grid (no upsampling, so the term measures the exporter, not the resize), failing when the minimum is below `PARITY_TOLERANCE['rf_detr_seg']['mask_iou']` (0.99 initial; the spike re-measures on both runtimes, Req 7.5).

**DD18 — The tensor tie-break for `C + 1 == 4` uses `detection.output_names`, then the runner's graph order with a warning, in both RF-DETR decoders.** `OnnxRunner.__call__` returns `session.run(None, feed)` in graph order (`inference_runtimes.py:386-389`), and rfdetr exports `dets` first; the manifest's `output_names` (from `training_metadata.onnx_output_names` or the inspector) is the authoritative order (Req 9.2). The fix to `RfDetrDetectionPostProcessor._split_outputs` is the same three lines; its tests gain the three-class case (Req 17.2).

**DD19 — Metric definitions are arch-keyed (`METRIC_DEFINITIONS_FOR_ARCH`) so YOLO/RF-DETR requests stay byte-identical** (Req 4.7): `yolo` and `rf_detr` map to the existing `DETECTION_METRIC_DEFINITIONS` object; `rf_detr_seg` maps to that list plus `test:mask-mAP50` and `test:mask-mAP50-95` regexes.

**DD20 — The frontend RLE codec moves to `utils/rle.ts` and is re-exported from `AnnotationCanvas.tsx`.** `encodeRleColumnMajor`, `parseRleCounts` and `decodeRleColumnMajor` (`edge-cv-portal/frontend/src/components/labeling/AnnotationCanvas.tsx:102-186`) are needed by the Portal `ResultsViewer` (Req 11.2); importing the canvas module into the viewer would pull the whole labeling UI. The functions move verbatim to `frontend/src/utils/rle.ts`, the canvas re-exports them so its tests and callers are unchanged, and `rleToImageData(rle, w, h, rgba)` is added beside them.

## Architecture

### 1. Shared layer: `detection_training.py` and `detector_conversion.py`

Pattern extended: the arch-keyed maps the RF-DETR spec added (`detection_training.py:42-146`) and the size tables of `detector_conversion.py:120-126` / `export_checkpoint.py:109-122`. No parallel module (Req 1).

`detection_training.py` additions, placed in the same blocks as their detection siblings:

```python
MODEL_TYPE_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION = 'instance_segmentation'          # beside :37
DETECTION_MODEL_TYPES = (MODEL_TYPE_OBJECT_DETECTION, MODEL_TYPE_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION)
DETECTION_ARCHES = ('yolo', 'rf_detr', 'rf_detr_seg')               # :42
RFDETR_SEG_INSTANCE_STAGE_TYPE = 'rf_detr_instance_segmentation'    # O16 a
RFDETR_SEG_SEMANTIC_STAGE_TYPE = 'rf_detr_semantic_segmentation'
STAGE_TYPE_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg'] = RFDETR_SEG_INSTANCE_STAGE_TYPE  # the default mode's stage type (Req 1.1)
OUTPUT_MODE_INSTANCE, OUTPUT_MODE_SEMANTIC = 'instance', 'semantic'
OUTPUT_MODES = (OUTPUT_MODE_INSTANCE, OUTPUT_MODE_SEMANTIC); DEFAULT_OUTPUT_MODE = OUTPUT_MODE_INSTANCE
ENTRY_POINT_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg'] = 'train_rfdetr_seg.py'; REQUIREMENTS_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg'] = 'requirements-rfdetr.txt'
SOURCEDIR_SIBLINGS_FOR_ARCH = {'yolo': (), 'rf_detr': (), 'rf_detr_seg': ('train_rfdetr.py',)}   # DD1
CHECKPOINT_MEMBER_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg'] = 'checkpoint_best_total.pth'
RFDETR_SEG_SIZES = {'nano': 312, 'small': 384, 'medium': 432, 'large': 504, 'xlarge': 624, '2xlarge': 768}   # D4
RFDETR_SEG_CLASS_FOR_SIZE = {'nano': 'RFDETRSegNano', ..., '2xlarge': 'RFDETRSeg2XLarge'}
RFDETR_SEG_QUERIES_FOR_SIZE = {'nano': 100, 'small': 100, 'medium': 200, 'large': 200, 'xlarge': 300, '2xlarge': 300}
RFDETR_SEG_INPUT_BLOCK_FOR_SIZE = {'nano': 12, 'small': 24, 'medium': 24, 'large': 24, 'xlarge': 24, '2xlarge': 24}
RFDETR_SEG_RESOLUTION_MIN, RFDETR_SEG_RESOLUTION_MAX = 240, 1152     # Req 1.2
RFDETR_SEG_MASK_DOWNSAMPLE = 4
RFDETR_SEG_DEFAULTS = {'rfdetr_seg_size': 'small', 'resolution': None, 'epochs': 100, 'batch': 4, 'grad_accum': 4,
                       'lr': 1e-4, 'patience': 10, 'score_threshold': 0.5, 'mask_threshold': 0.5, 'onnx_opset': 17}
DEFAULTS_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg'] = RFDETR_SEG_DEFAULTS
METRIC_DEFINITIONS_FOR_ARCH = {'yolo': DETECTION_METRIC_DEFINITIONS, 'rf_detr': DETECTION_METRIC_DEFINITIONS,
                               'rf_detr_seg': DETECTION_METRIC_DEFINITIONS + [mask-mAP50, mask-mAP50-95 regexes]}   # DD19
_ARTICLE_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg'] = 'an'
```

`output_mode` is deliberately **not** a key of `RFDETR_SEG_DEFAULTS`: `_reject_unknown` (`:260`) treats the defaults' keys as the accepted hyperparameter set, and Req 5.5 / 13.1 make `output_mode` a top-level request field. `DEFAULT_OUTPUT_MODE` sits in the same constant block, which is what Req 1.1's "Seg default set" denotes (see "Requirements corrections", C5).

Functions: `is_detr_family(arch) -> bool` (`arch in ('rf_detr', 'rf_detr_seg')`) replaces the `== 'rf_detr'` tests at `:281` (parser dispatch adds an `rf_detr_seg` branch first), `:420` (environment: the `rf_detr` dict with `RFDETR_SIZE` replaced by `RFDETR_SEG_SIZE` for Seg), `:590` (`iou_threshold` required only for non-DETR), `:606` (`normalize`), `:617` (`top_k`); `normalize_detection_arch` (`:215`) accepts the new key through `DETECTION_ARCHES`; `is_trained_detection_record` (`:173`) tests `model_type in DETECTION_MODEL_TYPES`; `stage_type_for(arch, output_mode=None)` (DD16); `rfdetr_seg_enabled()` (DD7); `_parse_rfdetr_seg_hyperparameters(raw)` beside `_parse_rfdetr_hyperparameters` (`:339`) — same shape, `rfdetr_seg_size ∈ RFDETR_SEG_SIZES`, resolution `None` → native, else a positive multiple of `RFDETR_SEG_INPUT_BLOCK_FOR_SIZE[size]` within the Seg bounds (message: `Invalid hyperparameter 'resolution': must be a multiple of <block> between 240 and 1152 for RF-DETR-Seg <size>`), `mask_threshold` strictly in (0, 1), no `iou_threshold` key; `detect_instance_attribute(entry)` (the `instance-segmentation` literal, or a GT attribute whose `-metadata.type` is `groundtruth/instance-segmentation`) beside `detect_bbox_attribute` (`:453`); `validate_detection_manifest_entry(entry, expected='bbox')` gains `expected='instance'` (Req 3.5): with `'bbox'` it behaves exactly as today; with `'instance'` it requires the instance attribute, returns `class_names` from its `class-map` and `kind: 'instance'`, and a bbox-only entry fails with "RF-DETR-Seg needs instance masks; this manifest has boxes only", an `anomaly-mask-ref` entry with "Manifest is a semantic segmentation manifest (anomaly-mask-ref); RF-DETR-Seg requires an instance-segmentation manifest" — never suggesting the Manifest Transformer (`:484-486`); `build_detection_device_manifest(..., output_mode=None, mask_threshold=None, output_names=None)` (§6); `build_sourcedir_tarball(code_dir, out_path, entry_point)` keeps its signature and adds the members of `_SIBLINGS_FOR_ENTRY_POINT[entry_point]` (the entry-point mirror of `SOURCEDIR_SIBLINGS_FOR_ARCH`, built like `_REQUIREMENTS_FOR_ENTRY_POINT` at `:649-651` and consulted beside `:678`; DD1); `resolve_base_model` needs no code change beyond the predicate and map extensions (`:722-833` is map-driven; the arch mismatch message uses `_ARTICLE_FOR_ARCH`), which is what Req 1.1's "pass without edit" cases pin.

`detector_conversion.py` (Req 1.4, 7): `RFDETR_PML_CLASSES = ('RFDETRXLarge', 'RFDETR2XLarge')` with a comment citing rfdetr 1.10.1 `detr.py:173, 193-197` and the PyPI README (D4); `RFDETR_SEG_SIZES`, `RFDETR_SEG_CLASS_FOR_SIZE`, `RFDETR_SEG_NATIVE_INPUTS`, `RFDETR_SEG_QUERIES_FOR_SIZE` beside `:121-126`; `INPUT_BOUNDS['rf_detr_seg'] = (240, 1152)` (error wording only — the `% INPUT_STEP` test is skipped for `rf_detr_seg`, Req 7.3); `DEFAULT_SCORE_THRESHOLD['rf_detr_seg'] = 0.5`, `DEFAULT_MASK_THRESHOLD = 0.5`; `MODEL_TYPE_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION`; `MODEL_TYPE_FOR_ARCH = {'yolo': object_detection, 'rf_detr': object_detection, 'rf_detr_seg': instance_segmentation}`; `is_detector_conversion_record` (`:615-624`) accepts both detection model types. `export_checkpoint.py` mirrors the tables (`RFDETR_SEG_SIZE_CLASSES`, `RFDETR_SEG_NATIVE_INPUTS`, corrected `RFDETR_PML_MODEL_NAMES`; the name gates `RFDETR_NON_DETECTION_PREFIXES = ('RFDETRKeypoint',)` and `RFDETR_REJECTED_SEG_NAMES = ('RFDETRSegPreview',)` land with §7's task gate, task 7.3, whose `startswith` fatal is their only consumer — implementation mismatch M-4); `train_rfdetr_seg.py` carries `SEG_SIZE_CLASSES` (§4). The identity test of Req 1.6, `tests/test_rfdetr_seg_vocabulary_parity.py`, imports the four modules (the two scripts via `importlib` from `datasets/`, as `test_train_rfdetr_static.py` does) and asserts the size, class, query and block tables and the two stage-type constants are equal.

### 2. Labeling: the `InstanceSegmentation` modality

Today: `VALID_MODALITIES = ('Classification', 'Segmentation', 'ObjectDetection')` (`edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/dda_labeling.py:273`), `AUTO_LABEL_MODEL_MODALITIES` (`:282`), `_validate_annotation` branches per modality (`:2622-2730`), pre-label payloads are `regions`/`boxes` (`:6379-6476`); the worker renders Segmentation regions into one PNG (`dda_labeling_worker.py:1069-1086`) and serialises through `dda_manifest.serialize_manifest` (`:1097`); the canvas types `LabelingModality` (`edge-cv-portal/frontend/src/components/labeling/AnnotationCanvas.tsx:62-66`) and keeps a label-indexed bitmap for Segmentation (`:404-414`). D3, O7 a, O8 a; Req 2.

**Backend (`dda_labeling.py`, `dda_labeling_worker.py`, `dda_autolabel_worker.py`, `dda_manifest.py`).** `MODALITY_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION = 'InstanceSegmentation'` joins `VALID_MODALITIES` and every enumeration the three modalities share — in the handler: job creation `:1690-1701`, the auto-label check `:1918-1920`, task fetch, submission `:2622`, the auto-label matrix `:282-287` for `sam`, `grounded-sam`, `llm` — not `bedrock`, Req 2.4; and in the **worker's manifest-generation gate**, which is modality-keyed in three places that would otherwise raise `KeyError` and fail every `InstanceSegmentation` job after labeling is complete: `REQUIRED_MANIFEST_ATTRIBUTES['InstanceSegmentation'] = ('source-ref', 'instance-segmentation', 'instance-segmentation-metadata')` (`dda_labeling_worker.py:214-222`), `GT_TASK_TYPES['InstanceSegmentation'] = 'instance-segmentation'` (`:223-227`), and `_validate_manifest_lines(lines, modality)` (`:1306-1405`, called unconditionally at `:1101` after `serialize_manifest` at `:1097`; `required = REQUIRED_MANIFEST_ATTRIBUTES[modality]` at `:1329`, `detect_ground_truth_attributes(entries[0], GT_TASK_TYPES[modality])` at `:1384-1385`), which gains an `InstanceSegmentation` branch mirroring the `ObjectDetection` one — `instance-segmentation` and `instance-segmentation-metadata` must both be objects (`:1370-1376` pattern) — and, in the final gate, an explicit `elif modality == 'InstanceSegmentation'` requiring `detected and detected.get('label_attr') == 'instance-segmentation'` (the else-branch at `:1398-1404` tests `label_attr != 'bounding-box'` and would flag the instance pair; `detect_ground_truth_attributes` with task type `'instance-segmentation'` returns `{label_attr: 'instance-segmentation', metadata_attr: 'instance-segmentation-metadata', segmentation_only: False}` because that key is the first non-skipped `-metadata` key, `manifest_transformer.py:26-63`). The Segmentation-only completion fields `mask_output_prefix`/`color_map` (`:1131-1135`) stay Segmentation-only. **Submit storage (DynamoDB 400 KB item limit).** The submit handler writes `Segmentation` annotations to S3 and stores only `annotation_s3_key` on the task item (`dda_labeling.py:2826-2853`; the `if job.get('task_type') == 'Segmentation'` is at `:2826`, its comment "region bitmaps (RLE JSON) go to S3" at `:2827`); every other modality is inline (`:2854-2856`). An instance annotation is the same payload class (up to 500 Canonical_RLE strings at source resolution), so the branch at `:2827` becomes `job.get('task_type') in ('Segmentation', 'InstanceSegmentation')` (the handler uses string literals today — `dda_labeling.py` defines `VALID_MODALITIES` at `:273` and no `MODALITY_*` constants; the new `MODALITY_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION` constant is introduced beside it and the literal form is shown here): instance annotations go to `labeling/<usecase>/<job>/annotations/<task>.json`, the item carries `annotation_s3_key`, an S3 failure returns the existing 500 and leaves the task Assigned; the worker's reader already accepts either form (`dda_labeling_worker.py:1213-1220`) and is unchanged. **Wire modality for pre-labels (one translation site).** The auto-label SQS message carries `'modality': job['task_type']` (`dda_labeling_worker.py:530`) and the consumer is `dda_autolabel_worker.py`, whose tuples `SAM_MODALITIES` / `GROUNDED_SAM_MODALITIES` (`:175-176`) gain `MODALITY_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION` (`BEDROCK_MODALITIES` at `:174` does not); a helper `_wire_modality(modality)` returning `MODALITY_SEGMENTATION` for `InstanceSegmentation` and the value itself otherwise is applied where the modality leaves the worker — the Grounded-SAM payload (`:969`), the LLM path's `generate_llm_prelabel(modality=…)` call (`:778-786`, `modality=` at `:782`), which drives `build_detection_prompt` and `guidance_to_prelabel` (`dda_llm_guidance.py:90`, `:787-808` branch on the three constants and raise `ValueError` for any other value), the SAM pre-label's `'modality'` field (`:887-892`; the SAM path returns class-`null` `regions` for either modality and sends no modality to its worker, `:849`), and the Grounded-SAM `modality == MODALITY_SEGMENTATION` builder (`:1004-1028`), so the stored pre-label is Segmentation-shaped (`regions`) and the Grounded-SAM worker contract is untouched (Req 2.4). The handler's Grounded-SAM **preview** path applies the same helper to its payload (`dda_labeling.py:6333`) and its `modality == 'Segmentation'` region check (`:6391`), and so does the LLM preview call (`:6157-6159`: `generate_llm_prelabel(modality=_wire_modality(run.get('task_type')))`, because `guidance_to_prelabel` raises `ValueError('unsupported modality …')` for anything but the three constants, `dda_llm_guidance.py:787-836`); the Grounded-SAM preview gate message (`:5239-5240`) names the three modalities (C18). The task-fetch code maps the stored Segmentation-shaped pre-label to `instances` (below). The annotation shape is Req 2.2's: `{"modality": "InstanceSegmentation", "image_size": {width, height}, "instances": [{"class", "rle", "box": {left, top, width, height}}], "classification"}`. `_validate_annotation`'s new branch (DD9): `instances` must be a list; each entry an object with `class` in the job's Label_Set (`null` is rejected at submit, as unclassified pre-label proposals are for Segmentation) and `rle` a string; the branch calls the new pure-Python helper `dda_manifest.tight_box_from_rle(rle, width, height) -> (left, top, width, height)`: it parses the counts with the same three `ValueError`s as `rle_decode` (`:205-230`: non-integer token, negative count, `sum(counts) != width * height` — factored into a shared `_rle_counts(rle, width, height)` that `rle_decode` now calls too, behaviour unchanged, its tests pass unmodified) and then derives the box from the **runs alone, without decoding a bitmap**: the counts are column-major with the background run first (`:40-44`), so a foreground run covering flat indices `[p0, p1]` spans columns `p0 // height .. p1 // height`; `left`/`right` are the min/max over runs, and the rows are `p0 % height .. p1 % height` when the run stays in one column and the full `0 .. height − 1` when it crosses a column boundary (the run then covers the bottom of one column and the top of the next). This is O(number of runs) in pure Python — cheaper than `rle_decode` itself and well within the Lambda budget for the ≤ 500 instances per image the validator caps — and raises `ValueError('no foreground pixels')` when every run is background. The validator maps these to `Instance <i>: rle does not match image_size` and `Instance <i> has no foreground pixels`, writes the derived box back into the stored annotation (the client-sent `box` is advisory only), and the serializer (§3), the worker's pre-label mapping and the tests share the one helper. `numpy` is **not** available to these Lambdas (`layers/imaging/requirements.txt` = `Pillow==12.3.0`; `numpy` only in `layers/video`, which `DdaLabelingHandler`/`DdaLabelingWorker` do not carry, `compute-stack.ts:2856, 2905`), and `dda_manifest.py` imports stdlib only (`:49-53`) — this helper keeps it that way. `classification` is derived as `anomaly` iff `instances` is non-empty, mirroring `_serialize_segmentation` (`dda_manifest.py:437-444`). Pre-labels: the task-fetch code maps a Segmentation-shaped pre-label payload (`regions: [{class, rle}]`, what the `sam`/`grounded-sam` worker returns per detection and what `dda_llm_guidance.py:633-700` rasterises for `llm`) to `instances` one-to-one with the derived box; the Grounded-SAM worker contract (`grounded-sam-autolabel` Req 4) is untouched, with `Segmentation` on the wire (Req 2.4). The worker's export branch for `InstanceSegmentation` builds the record `{modality, source_ref, image_size, instances, class_map, human_annotated, confidence}` (no PNG rendering, no `mask_s3_uri`), and `serialize_manifest` dispatches to `_serialize_instance_segmentation` (§3, Data contracts). The `skip_verification` / `human_annotated` / `confidence` markers (`dda_labeling_worker.py:1060-1067`) apply unchanged (Req 2.5). **Invariant (owned by the handler's job-creation validation, `dda_labeling.py`):** a skip-verification job is never an `InstanceSegmentation` job. Today this holds only indirectly: skip-verification requires a Bedrock model (`dda_labeling.py:1739-1744`, `bedrock_model_id` mandatory), `bedrock` is not offered for the new modality (`AUTO_LABEL_MODEL_MODALITIES`, `:282-287`, Req 2.4), and the worker refuses Bedrock generation for a modality outside `BEDROCK_MODALITIES` (`dda_autolabel_worker.py:641-644`, `GenerationFailure`) — but the handler checks the skip-verification `bedrock_model_id` only for presence, not against the modality matrix (the matrix check at `:1918-1920` covers the `auto_label` block only), so such a job could be *created* and fail at generation. The design therefore adds one validation error beside `:1739-1744`: `skip_verification` with `task_type == 'InstanceSegmentation'` → 400 `skip_verification: Skip-verification mode is not available for InstanceSegmentation (no Bedrock pre-labeler supports it)`. This matters because for skip-verification jobs the worker reads the **pre-label** as the annotation (`_load_task_annotation`, `dda_labeling_worker.py:1196-1211`, `prelabel_s3_key`), and with `_wire_modality` that payload is Segmentation-shaped (`regions`) while the export branch expects `instances`; the worker's `image_size` normaliser (`:1261-1268`) also covers only `ObjectDetection`/`Segmentation`. No `regions → instances` mapping is added to the worker in this spec; a later change that offers Bedrock (or any skip-verification source) for instances must add it, and `test_dda_labeling_instance.py` pins the invariant (an `InstanceSegmentation` job with `skip_verification: true` and a `bedrock_model_id` is rejected at creation with the new 400; the three existing modalities' skip-verification behaviour is unchanged). The SMGT path (`labeling.py:536-540`) is not extended; the wizard shows "available in DDA labeling only" for the new modality (Req 2.6).

**Frontend (`edge-cv-portal/frontend/src/components/labeling/AnnotationCanvas.tsx`, wizard, job list).** `LabelingModality` gains `'InstanceSegmentation'`. The canvas gains a polygon tool for that task type: `instances: EditableInstance[]` state (`{id, class, vertices: [x, y][], rle?: string, box?: Box, origin: 'prelabel' | 'user'}`), click-to-add vertices, click on the first vertex or double-click to close, drag a vertex to edit, select / reclass / delete through the instance rows (the pattern of the box rows, `:614-660`) plus an **instance undo stack** — new in this spec, the box tool has none at 43cd4ff1 (implementation mismatch M-5) — holding the instance list before each committed edit (add, reclass, delete, vertex drag) and, while a ring is being drawn, popping draft vertices first, each instance drawn with its class fill at 40 % alpha and an outline colour from a second palette indexed by instance position so two touching instances of one class remain distinguishable (Req 2.3). A polygon with fewer than three distinct vertices cannot be closed (the close affordance is disabled and a hint shown, Req 2.2). Instances may overlap. On save (the `onChange` path that builds the annotation, `:772-802` for Segmentation) each polygon is rasterised by an even-odd scanline fill into a per-instance `Uint8Array` and encoded with `encodeRleColumnMajor` (DD20); the tight box is computed client-side for display and sent, knowing the server recomputes it. A pre-label instance arrives with `rle` and no vertices: it is displayed from the decoded RLE as a filled mask and can be reclassed or deleted, but its outline is not editable (the polygon is lossy relative to the mask); the hint says so. The canvas's Clear_Prelabels control (testid `clear-prelabels`, `AnnotationCanvas.tsx:26-33`) removes the instances with `origin: 'prelabel'` exactly as it removes `prelabel-box-` boxes and prelabel-painted pixels today (a re-classed pre-label instance is still removed; user-drawn instances survive), taking the same full-state snapshot first so the Restore control (`restore-prelabels`) brings them back — `sam`/`grounded-sam`/`llm` proposals therefore behave like pre-label boxes; the property test `AnnotationCanvas.clearprelabels.property.test.tsx` gains an `InstanceSegmentation` case (random mix of prelabel/user instances: clear leaves exactly the user set, restore returns the original). The brush/eraser stay Segmentation-only (`:343-344`). The wizard (`pages/CreateLabelingJob.tsx`) lists the fourth option in `ALL_TASK_TYPE_OPTIONS` (`:202-206`) and offers it through the `taskTypeOptions` built under the `isDda` spread (`:671-677`) — the Requirement 2.6 carrier, so the SMGT path never sees it; the wizard also holds the **frontend mirror of `AUTO_LABEL_MODEL_MODALITIES`** — `SAM_MODALITIES` (`:85`), `LLM_MODALITIES` (`:87-91`) and `GROUNDED_SAM_MODALITIES` (`:97`) gain `'InstanceSegmentation'`, `BEDROCK_MODALITIES` (`:86`) does not — read by `isAutoLabelModelCompatible` (`:182-193`) and the auto-label option builders (`:577-612`), without which the wizard would offer no `sam` / `grounded-sam` / `llm` pre-labeler for the new modality and Requirement 2.4 would be unreachable from the UI; the Prompt_Tuning_Preview it renders for the `llm:` / `grounded-sam` families (`:2143-2160`) carries a second copy of the matrix (`components/labeling/PromptTuningPreview.tsx:150`, violation text `:338-341`) and a third hard-coded list in its `llm:` arm (`:356-360`, mismatch M-6) and `PreviewResultCanvas.tsx` draws `regions` only for `taskType === 'Segmentation'` (`:128, 169-173, 190`) — both are widened to the new modality (C18; the alternative of hiding the panel at `CreateLabelingJob.tsx:2143-2144` and keeping the backend gate refusing it is recorded in tasks.md 0.1 C18 and not taken, because Requirement 2.4 names the `llm` family's pre-labels). The job list "Task Type" column renders it; `04-labeling.md` is updated (§15). Tests (Req 2.7): `AnnotationCanvas.instance.test.tsx` (option, add/edit/close, select/reclass/delete/undo, rasterisation → RLE → `parseRleCounts` round trip including a border-touching instance, tight box, pre-label instances kept separate, two-vertex refusal) and backend `test_dda_labeling_instance.py` (modality accepted everywhere the three are; submit rejection of an empty instance; box recomputation; **submit → S3**: with moto S3 the annotation object is written under `labeling/<usecase>/<job>/annotations/<task>.json`, the item carries `annotation_s3_key` and no inline `annotation`, and an S3 failure leaves the task Assigned with the 500; `bedrock` not offered; `_wire_modality` sends `Segmentation` to the Grounded-SAM payload for an `InstanceSegmentation` job and the returned regions become one instance each; three existing modality suites unmodified, Req 2.1) plus a worker **generation** test in `test_dda_labeling_worker_instance.py`: a generated Instance_Manifest runs through `_validate_manifest_lines(lines, 'InstanceSegmentation')` with no issues, a line missing `instance-segmentation-metadata` is reported, and the three existing modalities' generation cases pass unmodified.

### 3. Dataset conversion: `datasets/manifest_to_detector_dataset.py`

Today: `detect_attribute` finds the bbox attribute (`datasets/manifest_to_detector_dataset.py:135-150`), `parse_entries` returns records `{'source_ref', 'file_name', 'width', 'height', 'boxes'}` (`:152-238`), `write_coco` emits `bbox`/`area`/`iscrowd` per box (`:383-391`), `--format coco|yolo` and `--coco-layout` (`:497-500`). rfdetr 1.10.1 decodes COCO `segmentation` RLE dicts (`coco.py:396-464`, WQ-req-author-1), drops `iscrowd == 1` (`:657`), and decodes masks only when the first kept annotation has a `segmentation` key (`:733`). Req 3; DD10.

`detect_attribute` gains a sibling `detect_instance_attribute(entry)` (the literal `instance-segmentation`, else a GT attribute whose `-metadata.type` is `groundtruth/instance-segmentation`). `parse_entries` tries the instance attribute first; when found, each annotation yields `(class_id, left, top, w, h, counts: List[int])` where `counts` is `rle.split()` parsed to ints and validated (`sum(counts) == width × height`, every count ≥ 0, at least one odd-indexed run > 0); an instance that fails is dropped with `WARN: <file_name> instance <i>: <reason>` and counted in `skipped['bad instance rle']`, and the record still carries its other instances (or zero → a negative, Req 3.3, 3.4). The record gains `'instances': [...]` and keeps `'boxes'` derived from the instances, so `group_by_similarity` (`:240`), `split_groups` (`:300`), `place_image` (`:339`) and `report` (`:440`) run unchanged (Req 3.4). A manifest mixing bbox-only and instance lines is rejected on the first line of the other kind (one format per manifest; the message names both line numbers).

`write_coco` emits, per instance, `{"id", "image_id", "category_id": cid + 1, "bbox": [left, top, w, h], "area": <foreground pixel count = sum(counts[1::2])>, "iscrowd": 0, "segmentation": {"counts": [...], "size": [height, width]}}`; it never emits an annotation without `segmentation` for an instance record. `write_yolo` exits `error: --format yolo cannot carry instance masks; use --format coco --coco-layout rfdetr` when any record has `instances` (DD10). The `--coco-layout rfdetr` tree shape (`train/`, `valid/`, `test/` with `_annotations.coco.json` per split) is unchanged. The size order `[height, width]` is COCO's and is what `pycocotools.frUncompressedRLE` reads (`_mask.pyx:270-286`); because `size` is taken from the dict, not from the image, a wrong `image_size` in the manifest would corrupt training silently — hence the `sum(counts)` assertion at write time and the serializer's `ValueError` at export time (§3 data contract).

Tests (Req 3.6, `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/test_manifest_to_detector_dataset.py`, which already imports the script from `datasets/`): RLE dict per instance equals the manifest's counts and `size == [h, w]`; `area` from mask pixels; the dropped-instance warning and `skipped` counter; a zero-instance image placed as a negative; identical split assignment for a bbox manifest and an instance manifest over the same images (same seed); the YOLO refusal; the mixed-format rejection.

### 4. Training entry point: `datasets/detection_training/train_rfdetr_seg.py`

Pattern extended: `train_rfdetr.py` (`SIZE_CLASSES :82-87`, `validate_resolution :137-146`, `build_model :315-351`, `train_kwargs :354-379`, `export_onnx :467-491`, `read_onnx_io :494-518`, `verify_two_outputs :532-595`, `verify_input :598-610`, `build_metadata :614-668`, `main :676-740`), `_common.py` (`cap_onnx_ir_version :377`, `write_metadata :409`, `fetch_base_weights :295`), `build_sourcedir.sh --arch` (`:21-24`). D2a, D4, O5 b, O6 a; Req 4; DD1.

```
datasets/detection_training/
├── train.py                  # YOLO (unchanged)
├── train_rfdetr.py           # RF-DETR detection (unchanged; its pure helpers are imported below)
├── train_rfdetr_seg.py       # NEW — RF-DETR-Seg: `import train_rfdetr as det`, `from _common import ...`
├── _common.py                # unchanged
├── requirements-rfdetr.txt   # shared by rf_detr and rf_detr_seg (rfdetr[train,onnx]==1.10.1 already covers the seg losses:
│                             #   the `train` extra is task-agnostic; the spike confirmed no missing extra, Req 4.1 — but one
│                             #   missing PIN: `transformers==5.17.0` added by task 0.3 / OR-1 (spike S5: rfdetr leaves
│                             #   transformers unpinned and the DLC resolved 5.24.0, whose public API lacks BackboneConfigMixin,
│                             #   so `import rfdetr` failed for Seg AND detection jobs; the Export_Image lock already pins 5.17.0)
└── build_sourcedir.sh        # --arch rf_detr_seg → train_rfdetr_seg.py + train_rfdetr.py + requirements-rfdetr.txt (as requirements.txt)
```

`train_rfdetr_seg.py` constants and pure helpers (all pinned by `test_train_rfdetr_seg_static.py`, libraries stubbed as in `test_train_rfdetr_static.py`):

```python
SEG_SIZE_CLASSES = {"nano": ("RFDETRSegNano", 312, 100, 12), "small": ("RFDETRSegSmall", 384, 100, 24),
                    "medium": ("RFDETRSegMedium", 432, 200, 24), "large": ("RFDETRSegLarge", 504, 200, 24),
                    "xlarge": ("RFDETRSegXLarge", 624, 300, 24), "2xlarge": ("RFDETRSeg2XLarge", 768, 300, 24)}
                    # (class, native resolution, num_queries, input block); pinned to detection_training.RFDETR_SEG_* (Req 1.6)
DEFAULT_SIZE = "small"; RESOLUTION_MIN, RESOLUTION_MAX = 240, 1152; MASK_DOWNSAMPLE = 4
STAGE_TYPE = "rf_detr_instance_segmentation"                 # the default mode's stage type (hints only; the record decides)
DEVICE_SCORE_THRESHOLD = DEVICE_MASK_THRESHOLD = 0.5
ONNX_MASKS_NAME = "masks"                                     # + det.ONNX_BOXES_NAME / det.ONNX_LOGITS_NAME
METRIC_KEYS = det.METRIC_KEYS + ("test_mask_map50", "test_mask_map50_95")
_SEG_EVAL_KEY_MAP = {**det._EVAL_KEY_MAP, "segm_mAP_50": "test_mask_map50", "segm_mAP_50_95": "test_mask_map50_95"}  # rfdetr 1.10.1 coco_eval.py:128 (WQ-design-author-2)

def validate_seg_resolution(size, resolution) -> int      # pure: multiple of SEG_SIZE_CLASSES[size][3] within bounds, else sys.exit(FATAL …)
def stamp_static_output_dims(path) -> Dict[str, List[int]]  # M-1 (spike S2): run the export once on zeros in the job's onnxruntime, write the
                                                          # observed output shapes into graph.output (apply_output_dims, pure over the protobuf
                                                          # dims: dim_param cleared, dim_value set; rank mismatch -> ValueError), onnx.checker, save
def verify_seg_outputs(outputs, num_classes, resolution) -> (Q, boxes, logits, masks, names_in_order)   # pure, Req 4.5
def build_seg_metadata(cfg, class_names, metrics, input_shape, shapes, names, top_k, base) -> dict        # pure, Req 4.6
def seg_metrics_from_eval(result, split="test") -> dict   # pure: det.metrics_from_eval's loop over _SEG_EVAL_KEY_MAP; missing key → NaN + WARN, Req 4.7
```

`read_config` reads the detection environment plus `RFDETR_SEG_SIZE` (default `small`) and derives `model_class`, `native`, `top_k` and `block` from `SEG_SIZE_CLASSES`; `RESOLUTION` absent → native; `validate_seg_resolution` runs before any download, and rfdetr's own `ValueError` at construction (`detr.py:388-394`) is the backstop (Req 4.4). The Seg script defines its **own** `stage_dataset(cfg)` — `det.stage_dataset` (`train_rfdetr.py:213-226`) is not called, because it has no parameter for a first-line check and performs none — composed of the same helpers in the same order plus one local step: `_common.stage_manifest_and_images(cfg.manifest_s3, cfg.images_s3, WORK)` → local `first_line_is_instance_manifest(first_line)` (true when the line carries the `instance-segmentation` attribute or a `-metadata.type == groundtruth/instance-segmentation` companion; otherwise `FATAL: manifest line 1 carries no instance-segmentation attribute (bbox-only or anomaly-mask-ref manifests cannot train RF-DETR-Seg)`, Req 4.2) → `det.run_converter(manifest, images, WORK / "dataset", "coco", coco_layout="rfdetr")` → `det.assert_splits(dataset, ("train", "valid"))` (names a missing/empty split) → the same `WARN: no test split` line → `det.read_class_names(dataset)` (fatal on an empty category list, as `:224-225`). `build_model` is `det.build_model` with the Seg class (`pretrain_weights` + `trust_checkpoint=True` + `num_classes` pin on the base-weights path, published Seg COCO weights otherwise, Req 4.3); `train_kwargs` is `det.train_kwargs` unchanged — the seg loss knobs stay at `SegmentationTrainConfig` defaults and are not passed (Req 4.3; `TrainConfig` is `extra="forbid"`, so adding keys that may not exist is the only risk, avoided by passing none). Metrics follow the precedent's mapping pattern exactly (`train_rfdetr.py:118-125` `_EVAL_KEY_MAP`, `metrics_from_eval(result, split)` at `:418-434`, `read_test_metrics` at `:435-460`): rfdetr 1.10.1's `.evaluate()` returns a flat `dict[str, float]` keyed `"<split>/<metric>"` and the only mask keys are `{split}/segm_mAP_50_95` and `{split}/segm_mAP_50` (`coco_eval.py:128`, `:870-873`; `web-answers.md` WQ-design-author-2, settled at source — there is no `mask_mAP_*` key and no `segm` sub-dict), so the Seg file defines `_SEG_EVAL_KEY_MAP = {**det._EVAL_KEY_MAP, "segm_mAP_50": "test_mask_map50", "segm_mAP_50_95": "test_mask_map50_95"}` and `seg_metrics_from_eval(result, split) -> Dict[str, float]`, the same split-prefixing loop as `det.metrics_from_eval` (`result.get(f"{split}/{src}")`, then `result.get(src)`) over `_SEG_EVAL_KEY_MAP`, returning the six `METRIC_KEYS` in order. A missing or non-numeric key becomes `NaN` — the precedent's rule (`:431-432`), kept because the `MetricDefinitions` regex `[0-9.eE+-]+` does not match `NaN`, so the metric is simply absent in SageMaker and Training Detail instead of a fake `0.0` — and the Seg loop additionally prints one `WARN: evaluate() returned no <split>/<key>` line per missing key so the absence is visible in the job log (Req 4.7). `read_test_metrics` is the Seg file's own copy of `det.read_test_metrics` calling `seg_metrics_from_eval` (the precedent function hard-codes `metrics_from_eval`; `train_rfdetr.py` stays untouched, DD1); its `WARN: <split>-split evaluation failed` catch-all is kept, and the raw `rfdetr evaluate(<split>): {...}` log line (`:455`) is what spike item S3 reads. `evaluate()` rebuilds the model as a Lightning module and reloads the state dict (`detr.py:1228-1252`), which needs the `train` extra — already in `rfdetr[train,onnx]==1.10.1` (WQ-design-author-2). `METRIC_DEFINITIONS_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg']` (DD19) appends `{'Name': 'test:mask-mAP50', 'Regex': r'"test_mask_map50": ([0-9.eE+-]+)'}` and `{'Name': 'test:mask-mAP50-95', 'Regex': r'"test_mask_map50_95": ([0-9.eE+-]+)'}` to the detection list; the static test runs every `rf_detr_seg` regex against a Seg `TEST METRICS` line and asserts each box regex still matches only its own key (`"test_map50": ` is not a substring of `"test_mask_map50": `).

`verify_seg_outputs` (Req 4.5): exactly three outputs, each a static int shape with batch 1; one rank-3 with last dim 4 (boxes), one rank-3 with last dim `S` (logits), one rank-4 `[1, Q, Hm, Wm]` (masks) with `Hm == Wm == resolution // 4`; all three share `Q`; `S == num_classes + 1` exactly (an `S == num_classes` graph fails with the `verify_two_outputs` message at `train_rfdetr.py:586-590`); when `S == 4` the names `dets`/`labels` settle boxes vs logits, and a graph with `S == 4` and no usable names fails `FATAL: ONNX outputs [...] are ambiguous (two [1, Q, 4] tensors, no dets/labels names)`. It returns the graph's output **order** as `names_in_order` (`read_onnx_io` already returns `(name, shape)` pairs in graph order), which `build_seg_metadata` writes as `onnx_output_names` and the packager copies into `detection.output_names` (Req 6.1, 9.2; DD18). Every other count or shape fails with a `FATAL:` quoting every output.

`main` follows `det.main` (`:676-740`): export with `det.export_onnx` (static batch 1, `dynamic_batch=False`, `output_name="model"`), **`stamp_static_output_dims(dst)`** (M-1, task 0.3: the raw 1.10.1 export declares the four `masks` dims symbolically — `Addmasks_dim_0..3`, spike S2 — and onnxruntime 1.19.2 reports those names from `get_outputs()`, so without the stamp `read_onnx_io` would hand `verify_seg_outputs` a non-static mask shape; the stamp runs the graph once on a zero `[1, 3, R, R]` tensor, writes the observed shapes into `graph.output`, re-validates with `onnx.checker` and saves — IR unchanged, outputs byte-identical, verified live in 1.30.0 / 1.19.2 / 1.16.3; a stamp failure is `FATAL: could not stamp static output dims …`), `cap_onnx_ir_version(dst)` (≤ 10, `_common.py:374-377`), `read_onnx_io`, `det.verify_input`, `verify_seg_outputs` (no `dim_param` tolerance: after the stamp a symbolic dim is a defect), `write_metadata`, copy `checkpoint_best_total.pth` to `/opt/ml/model`, print `DONE`. `build_seg_metadata` writes the Req 4.6 keys (§Data contracts, "training_metadata.json (Seg)"). The `TEST METRICS` line carries the six keys and `METRIC_DEFINITIONS_FOR_ARCH['rf_detr_seg']` lifts them (DD19).

### 5. Portal training launch: `training.py`

Today: `valid_model_types` (`edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/training.py:800-801`), the `object_detection` branch to `_create_detection_training_job` (`:824-835`), arch/parser (`:480-485`), `network_input`/`published_ref` (`:488-491`), `MetricDefinitions` (`:602`), the per-arch record (`:630-668`). Req 5; O4 b, O5 b, O13 a, O15 a.

Request handling in `create_training_job` (the `POST /training` handler): `valid_model_types` gains `MODEL_TYPE_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION`; immediately after the model-type check and **before** `check_user_access`, `if model_type == MODEL_TYPE_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION and not rfdetr_seg_enabled(): return create_response(403, {'error': 'RF-DETR-Seg is disabled on this portal'})` (Req 15.2: "before any validation" of the Seg request body; the model-type check itself is the dispatch, not Seg validation). The branch at `:824` becomes `if model_type in DETECTION_MODEL_TYPES:` and `_create_detection_training_job` receives `model_type`. Inside it, step 1 adds the cross-check before parsing: `detection_arch = normalize_detection_arch(body.get('detection_arch') or ('rf_detr_seg' if model_type == MODEL_TYPE_INSTANCE_SEGMENTATION else None))`; `model_type == instance_segmentation and detection_arch != 'rf_detr_seg'` → 400 `instance_segmentation requires detection_arch rf_detr_seg`; `detection_arch == 'rf_detr_seg' and model_type != instance_segmentation` → 400 `detection_arch rf_detr_seg requires model_type instance_segmentation` (Req 5.1). `output_mode = str(body.get('output_mode') or DEFAULT_OUTPUT_MODE).lower()`; not in `OUTPUT_MODES` → 400 naming the field; for non-Seg archs a present `output_mode` is rejected with 400 (`output_mode applies to instance_segmentation only`) so YOLO/RF-DETR requests cannot carry a silent no-op key. `network_input = params['resolution'] if is_detr_family(arch) else params['imgsz']`; `published_ref = params['rfdetr_seg_size'] if arch == 'rf_detr_seg' else (params['rfdetr_size'] if arch == 'rf_detr' else params['base_weights'])`. The manifest check calls `validate_detection_manifest_entry(first_line, expected='instance' if arch == 'rf_detr_seg' else 'bbox')` (Req 3.5, 5.1). `MetricDefinitions=METRIC_DEFINITIONS_FOR_ARCH[detection_arch]` (Req 4.7). The Seg record block (`:630` sibling) is the `rf_detr` block with `rfdetr_seg_size` instead of `rfdetr_size`, `mask_threshold` added, `top_k = RFDETR_SEG_QUERIES_FOR_SIZE[size]`, `output_mode`, and no `iou_threshold` (Req 5.5); the record's `model_type` is `instance_segmentation`, `runtime = 'onnx'` — the record `model_type` (`training.py:688`) and the audit `model_type` (`:721`) write the `model_type` the handler passes in (`object_detection` for `yolo` / `rf_detr`, so those records are byte-identical), not the `MODEL_TYPE_OBJECT_DETECTION` literal (C33 iv). `resolve_base_model(kind, ref, 'rf_detr_seg', …)` works through the extended maps (§1); the `training_job` branch additionally requires the base record's `model_type == instance_segmentation` (it does through `is_trained_detection_record` plus the arch comparison, which rejects a detection checkpoint with "is a rf_detr detector; cannot start an rf_detr_seg run from it", Req 5.4). `instance_type` stays the request field defaulting to `DETECTION_DEFAULT_INSTANCE_TYPE` (Req 5.3); the frontend pre-selects `ml.g5.xlarge` for large/xlarge/2xlarge (§12).

**LFV-side rejection of an Instance_Manifest (Req 3.2, 3.5).** The Marketplace path validates with `validate_marketplace_manifest(manifest_uri, usecase, model_type)` (`training.py:147-300`, called at `:843`). Today an Instance_Manifest line reaches the required-attribute scan (`:222-234`), misses `anomaly-label`/`anomaly-mask-ref`, sets `has_gt_pattern` because `instance-segmentation-metadata` ends in `-metadata` (`:238-239`), and `detect_ground_truth_attributes(first_entry, 'segmentation')` returns a dict (`manifest_transformer.py:36-63`: `label_attr = 'instance-segmentation'` is present in the entry), so the response is "Manifest is in Ground Truth format and requires transformation … Use the Data Management > Transform Manifest tool" (`:250-261`) — the dead end 3.2/3.5 forbid, naming no modality. The validator gains **one early return** before the required-attribute scan (`:222`), in the shape of the task-type mismatch check at `:282-290`: `if 'instance-segmentation' in first_entry or 'instance-segmentation-metadata' in first_entry: return {'valid': False, 'errors': ['Manifest is an instance-segmentation manifest; it trains RF-DETR-Seg (model_type instance_segmentation), not the Marketplace classification/segmentation model.'], 'message': 'Task type mismatch: manifest is instance segmentation', 'detected_attributes': list(first_entry.keys())}` — no `transformation_needed`, no `suggestion`. No classification or segmentation manifest carries either key, so the existing LFV cases cannot reach it. The reverse rejection (a semantic or bbox manifest offered to Seg training) is `validate_detection_manifest_entry(expected='instance')` (§1).

Preservation: the LFV branches below `:835` are untouched **except** this one early return in `validate_marketplace_manifest`, which no classification/segmentation manifest can reach; YOLO/RF-DETR request bodies to SageMaker are byte-identical because the metric map returns the same list object and the environment dicts are unchanged (Req 17.1). Tests (Req 5.7) in `tests/test_detection_training_create.py` siblings: the Seg request shape (`sagemaker_program = train_rfdetr_seg.py`, env keys incl. `RFDETR_SEG_SIZE`, no `IOU`, no thresholds), each 400 of Req 5.1/5.2/5.4 and the `output_mode` 400s, the record fields of 5.5, the Seg_Switch 403 before access check, the `validate_marketplace_manifest` early return for an Instance_Manifest offered as `model_type = 'segmentation'` (400 naming `instance_segmentation`, no `transformation_needed` key; Req 3.6), and the existing preservation cases unmodified.

### 6. Device manifest and packaging: `build_detection_device_manifest`, `packaging.py`

Pattern extended: `build_detection_device_manifest` (`detection_training.py:548-642`), `package_trained_detection_component` (`packaging.py:361-540`: arch `:436-438`, thresholds/hints `:455-462`, `is_rf_detr` branch `:464-477`, real shapes `:497-510`, stage dir `:517`), `tests/test_detection_manifest_parity.py` (rf_detr cases `:99-186`). D1, O1 a, O15 a, O16 a; Req 6; DD6, DD16.

`build_detection_device_manifest(..., detection_arch, top_k, output_mode=None, mask_threshold=None, output_names=None)`: for `rf_detr_seg` the mode defaults to `DEFAULT_OUTPUT_MODE`, `mask_threshold` to 0.5; `preserve_aspect` defaults to `False` as for `rf_detr`; `iou_threshold` is not required (`:590` uses `is_detr_family`). The stage is the `rf_detr` stage with `type = stage_type_for('rf_detr_seg', output_mode)` and `normalize: True`; the **top-level** `detection` block is the `rf_detr` block (`layout: 'rf_detr'`, `num_classes`, `score_threshold`, `network_input`, `preserve_aspect`, `top_k`) plus `mask_threshold`, `output_mode`, `output_names` (when given) and, last, `class_names` (when given) — top-level in both modes, never stage-level (Req 6.1; the base model merges it into the stage with `setdefault`, `lfv_model_template.py:528-535`). `model_graph.pixel_level_classes = {'names': ['background'] + class_names_or_placeholders, 'normal_ids': [0]}` with exactly `num_classes + 1` names in both modes (placeholders `class_i` only when the trainer path has no names). In instance mode `task: 'object_detection'`; in semantic mode the `task` key is omitted (anomaly default). Key order is fixed by construction (`runtime`, `runtime_artifact`, `model_graph`, `input_shape`, `preprocessing`, [`task`], `detection`, `dataset`), matching the detection builder (`:620-641`).

`generate_dda_package` (DD6): the `is_rf_detr_seg` branch (`model_converter.py:993-1015`) is replaced by `manifest = dt.build_detection_device_manifest(..., detection_arch='rf_detr_seg', output_mode=output_mode, mask_threshold=mask_threshold, output_names=output_names, top_k=num_queries); manifest.pop('dataset')` and the real three shapes written into `stage.output_shape` (logits) / `stage.output_shapes` (all three) as the packager does. `test_detection_manifest_parity.py` gains `test_rfdetr_seg_trained_manifest_equals_smart_import_manifest` (parametrised over both modes, six sizes, with/without names) asserting dict equality **and** `json.dumps` byte equality (key order), plus `'detection' not in manifest['model_graph']['stages'][0]` and the `pixel_level_classes` length (Req 6.2).

`package_trained_detection_component`: `is_detr = is_detr_family(detection_arch)`; `is_seg = detection_arch == 'rf_detr_seg'`. The `is_rf_detr` branch at `:464-477` becomes the DETR-family branch and, for Seg, reads `output_mode = det.get('output_mode') or hints.get('output_mode') or DEFAULT_OUTPUT_MODE` (overridable by the Package request body's `output_mode`, Req 6.4), `mask_threshold = det.get('mask_threshold', hints.get('mask_threshold', 0.5))`, `output_names = meta.get('onnx_output_names') or det.get('output_names')`, `top_k = meta.get('top_k') or det.get('top_k') or hints.get('top_k')`. The shape block at `:502-510` requires `len(real_shapes) == 3` for Seg (two entries → `PackagingError`-style 400 `Seg record has a two-output artifact: <shapes>`; a detection artifact mislabelled as Seg, Req 6.3): `stage0['output_shape'] = logits_shape`, `stage0['output_shapes'] = [boxes, logits, masks]`. For Seg the guard is `_is_int_shape(boxes, 3) and _is_int_shape(logits, 3) and _is_int_shape(masks, 4)` — `_is_int_shape(shape, rank)` a rank-parametrised sibling of `_is_3d_int_shape` (`:355-358`, which stays unchanged for the YOLO / RF-DETR branches; at 43cd4ff1 the block is wrapped in `if isinstance(real_shapes, list) and len(real_shapes) == 2 and all(_is_3d_int_shape(s) for s in real_shapes)`, `:504-505`, so a branch that only changed `2` → `3` would silently skip the rank-4 mask shape with no 400 — C29) — with the three entries identified by shape (the rank-3 entry with `s[2] == 4` → boxes, the other rank-3 entry → logits, the rank-4 entry → masks), never by position; the three-class record (two `[1, Q, 4]` entries) is settled by the aligned `onnx_output_names` and, nameless, by list order — never a 400, the shapes being equal (C33 i); a two-entry list, a wrong-rank entry, a non-int dim or a missing `onnx_output_shapes` on a Seg record → the 400 quoting the shapes. The stage dir is `stage_type_for(detection_arch, output_mode)` (`:517`).
