After the component is registered the record gains `packaged_output_mode` beside `packaged_components` (`:1102`-style update), read by Model Detail (Req 12.3). `output_mode` is resolved and `packaged_output_mode` written **only when `is_seg`** — a conditional second `SET` clause on the two `update_item` sites (`packaging.py:1286-1290` trained records, `:1100-1104` re-packaged Conversion_Records) and a trailing `plan_finalize_transition(..., packaged_output_mode=None)` keyword (`detector_conversion.py:691-697`) that adds the field only when given; `yolo` / `rf_detr` records keep today's `UpdateExpression`, attribute set and finalize `set_fields` byte-for-byte, and `build_detection_device_manifest` receives `output_mode=None` for `rf_detr` (C32: those sites are shared by every arch, so read literally an `rf_detr` record would have gained `packaged_output_mode: 'instance'` — a new attribute on a Requirement 17.1 surface with no test to catch it). The pass-down mechanism: `package_trained_detection_component(…, output_mode=None)` (one trailing keyword, `str` return kept); `package_components` / `package_detector_conversion` resolve the Seg mode once through the pure `effective_output_mode(det, hints=None, override=None)` (`override → det.output_mode → hints.output_mode → DEFAULT_OUTPUT_MODE`), pass it down and persist the same value as `packaged_output_mode` (C32). The audit literals `packaging.py:1109` / `:1295` `details['model_type'] = 'object_detection'` become `training_job.get('model_type') or 'object_detection'` (C33 iii). `package_components` (`:1125`) parses `body.get('output_mode')` for Seg records only: when present it is validated (`OUTPUT_MODES`, else 400), gated by the Seg_Switch (403 when OFF; an absent `output_mode` packages the stored mode even when OFF, Req 15.2), and passed down. Conversion_Records reach the same function via `package_detector_conversion` (`:1244`), so Req 6.6 needs no second packager; `is_detector_conversion_record` accepts `instance_segmentation` (§1). `package_onnx_component` (`:224-336`) is unchanged — it nests under `stages[0].type`, which is already the Seg_Stage_Type for imports (Req 8.6); because it never rebuilds the manifest, an `output_mode` override on an imported ONNX Seg record is refused with the C34 400 (DD16) and an absent one writes no `packaged_output_mode`. `packaging.py` is rebaselined in `iam_out_of_scope_baseline.json` (Req 6.5, 17.4).

Tests: `test_detection_training_compile_package.py` siblings for a Seg record (ZIP layout `rf_detr_instance_segmentation/model.onnx` or `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation/model.onnx` per mode, manifest fields, the three-shape rule, the two-shape 400, the `output_mode` override and its persistence, the 403 when OFF with an override and the success without one), the YOLO/RF-DETR cases unmodified (Req 6.5).

### 7. Checkpoint probe and `.pth` conversion

Pattern extended: `checkpoint_probe.classify_checkpoint` (`rfdetr_checkpoint` branch `checkpoint_probe.py:547-581`), `detector_conversion.py` (`_rfdetr_size_from_evidence :197-231`, `assess_checkpoint :233`, `validate_conversion_request :358-460`, `build_conversion_record :537`, `check_onnx_against_record :1076`), `export_checkpoint.py` (`convert_rfdetr :837-921`, `compare_rfdetr_parity :621`, `verify_rfdetr_contract :923`, `build_conversion_metadata :943`, `PARITY_TOLERANCE :146-150`), the Export_Image. D2b, D4, O5 b, O6 a, O10 a, O15 a; Req 7; DD17.

**Probe.** In the `rfdetr_checkpoint` branch the state-dict scan adds `seg_keys = [k for k in sd if k.startswith(_RFDETR_SEG_HEAD_PREFIXES)]` with `_RFDETR_SEG_HEAD_PREFIXES = ('segmentation_head.',)` — settled at source: `LWDETR.__init__` stores the head as `self.segmentation_head` (rfdetr 1.10.1 `lwdetr.py:155`; its tensors are `segmentation_head.blocks.*`, `.spatial_features_proj.*`, `.query_features_block.*`, `.query_features_proj.*`, `.bias`; no `mask_` prefix exists anywhere, so the `mask_` half of `export_checkpoint.py:854` matches nothing and is dropped; WQ-design-author-1). The probe records **two task signals** and the picks that tell the sizes apart: `ev['rfdetr_signals']['segmentation_head_keys'] = len(seg_keys)` and `ev['rfdetr_signals']['args_segmentation_head'] = args.get('segmentation_head')` (the flag rfdetr's own loader decides seg-vs-detection from, `utilities/state_dict.py:375-395`; `_namespace_fields` at `:546` already yields dict and `Namespace` args alike), and the `args_picks` loop (`:574-575`) gains `segmentation_head`, `patch_size`, `num_windows` and `dec_layers` beside `encoder`, `resolution`, `num_queries`, `group_detr`, `num_classes`. `ev['rfdetr_task'] = 'segmentation'` when either signal is true (`seg_keys` non-empty **or** `args_segmentation_head is True`), else `'detection'`; a disagreement (one **true**, one **false**) is recorded as `ev['rfdetr_signals']['task_signals_disagree'] = True` and still classified by the state-dict signal, and the Conversion_Job's task gate (below) requires the state-dict signal, so a stripped or foreign file cannot pass on `args` alone. **Spike S1 / S1b amendment (task 0.3, M-2):** the state dict is the authoritative task signal and `args_segmentation_head` is **`None` for every checkpoint seen live** — the six published Seg weights are stripped `{"model"}`-only files (no `args`, `model_name` or `rfdetr_version`; `zip_root = checkpoint_best_regular_stripped`) and a 1.10.1 PTL training checkpoint's `args` is the serialised **TrainConfig** (94 fields: `lr`, `epochs`, `dataset_dir`, `class_names`, …) without `segmentation_head`, `patch_size`, `num_windows`, `resolution`, `num_queries`, `encoder`, `num_classes` or `dec_layers`; those model fields live in a `model_config` dict that only the EMA file carries. So `None` (absent) is **never** a disagreement — only an explicit `False` beside a populated mask head is — and the `args_picks` loop reads each pick from `args` first and from `model_config` second when the checkpoint carries one (a third optional source beside `model_name`); `rfdetr_seg_size` stays `None` for a published Seg file (no size signal but tensor shapes; 7.2's unknown-size path — the user's native input through `RFDETR_SEG_SIZE_FOR_NATIVE` and the job's shell-signature match — covers it), while `model_name` carries the size for DDA-trained checkpoints. rfdetr's own loader behaves the same way (`validate_checkpoint_compatibility` skips the `segmentation_head` check when `args` lacks it and infers `patch_size` from `backbone.0.encoder.encoder.embeddings.patch_embeddings.projection.weight`, a `[hidden, 3, 12, 12]` tensor for every Seg size). `model_name` is **optional**: PTL training checkpoints carry it since rfdetr 1.7.0, `export_for_roboflow` files (`{"model", "args"}` only, `detr.py:2890`) do not (WQ-design-author-1), so the task and the size never depend on it — `patch_size == 12` (every Seg size) versus 16 for the Nano/Small/Medium detection configs (`config.py:801-837`; WQ-design-author-4 read only those three, plus `RFDETRBaseConfig`'s default of 14 at `:772`; the current `RFDETRLargeConfig` at `:838-860` was not read) is a cheap corroborating signal recorded in `args_picks`, never gated on. `result['arch'] = 'rf_detr_seg' if seg task else 'rf_detr'` (Req 7.1, 7.7). `num_classes` still comes from `class_embed.bias.shape[0] - 1` (`:564-567`). `kind` stays `rfdetr_checkpoint`; the never-raise contract is kept (the scan is over keys already in memory). `tests/fixtures/checkpoints/builders.py` gains `rfdetr_seg_checkpoint(..., envelope=...)` (the detection builder plus the 35 `segmentation_head.*` tensors — `bias`, `blocks.{0..3}.{dwconv,norm,pwconv1}.{weight,bias}`, `query_features_block.{layers.0,layers.2,norm_in}.{weight,bias}`, `query_features_proj.*`, `spatial_features_proj.*` — in the **four envelopes the spike recorded** (M-2): stripped `{"model"}` only (published weights: no `args`, `model_name`, `rfdetr_version`), DDA-trained `checkpoint_best_total.pth` (`args` = a TrainConfig dump with `class_names` and no model fields, `model_name = 'RFDETRSegNano'`, `rfdetr_version = '1.10.1'`, `state_dict` with the PTL `model.` prefix beside `model`), the EMA file (the same plus `model_config` with `num_queries`, `resolution`, `patch_size`, `num_windows`, `mask_downsample_ratio`, `num_classes`) and the legacy `export_for_roboflow` shape `{"model", "args"}` with `args.segmentation_head = True`, `args.patch_size = 12` — each a test case; the published envelope asserts `rfdetr_task = 'segmentation'`, `args_segmentation_head = None`, no `task_signals_disagree` and `rfdetr_seg_size = None` at assessment). `ARCHES` (`checkpoint_probe.py:104`, exported at `:93`, consulted by the membership checks `:79` and `:243`) gains `'rf_detr_seg'` — `("yolo", "rf_detr", "rf_detr_seg", None)` — in the same commit, which `result['arch'] = 'rf_detr_seg'` forces; `KINDS` and `FINE_TUNABLE_KINDS` are unchanged; its exact pin `tests/test_checkpoint_probe.py:100` (`test_vocabulary_matches_spike_contract`) moves (C22).

**Assessment and request validation.** `_rfdetr_size_from_evidence` returns `(size, reason, task)`; it stops rejecting names containing `seg` (`:209-210`), rejects `RFDETRSegPreview` ("deprecated preview size; re-export with a current Seg size") and names containing `keypoint`, keeps the encoder gate at `:214-218` **unchanged** — every `RFDETRSeg*Config` inherits `encoder = "dinov2_windowed_small"` from `RFDETRBaseConfig` (`config.py:772`) and overrides nothing about the backbone but `patch_size` and `num_windows`, and the only `dinov2_windowed_base` config is the legacy `RFDETRLargeDeprecatedConfig` (`config.py:789-798`), whose model class `RFDETRLargeDeprecated` is already in `RFDETR_LEGACY_CLASSES` (`detector_conversion.py:125`) (WQ-design-author-4, settled at source) — and infers the size with `model_name` **optional** (WQ-design-author-1): first `model_name` against `RFDETR_SEG_CLASS_FOR_SIZE`, else `args_picks.resolution` against `RFDETR_SEG_SIZES` (312 / 384 / 432 / 504 / 624 / 768 are pairwise distinct, so resolution alone resolves the pairs that share a query count — Nano/Small `Q = 100`, Medium/Large 200, XLarge/2XLarge 300 — and `encoder` cannot help because it is identical for all six), with `args_picks.num_queries` checked against `RFDETR_SEG_QUERIES_FOR_SIZE[size]` as a consistency reason when both are present. The task is decided **before** the size table is chosen (384 is shared between detection nano and Seg small), so a 384 checkpoint with no `model_name` goes to the Seg table iff `evidence.rfdetr_task == 'segmentation'`. `assess_checkpoint` maps a Seg task to `arch = 'rf_detr_seg'`, `model_type = 'instance_segmentation'`, `rfdetr_seg_size`, `num_queries = RFDETR_SEG_QUERIES_FOR_SIZE[size]` and the pre-fill `network_input = native` (Req 7.2); the corrected PML list makes `RFDETRSegXLarge`/`RFDETRSeg2XLarge` convertible (D4). `validate_conversion_request`: `model_type` must equal `MODEL_TYPE_FOR_ARCH[arch]` (400 naming both); for `rf_detr_seg` the `INPUT_STEP`/`INPUT_BOUNDS` gate at `:386-389` is skipped and the native-only rule at `:391-398` applies with `RFDETR_SEG_SIZES`/`RFDETR_SEG_NATIVE_INPUTS`; `score_threshold` and `mask_threshold` in (0, 1) (defaults 0.5/0.5); `class_names` count = `num_classes`, required when the checkpoint carries none; `output_mode ∈ OUTPUT_MODES` (default instance); `iou_threshold` present → 400 `'iou_threshold' does not apply to RF-DETR-Seg` (the `:420-425` branch generalised with `is_detr_family`, Req 7.3). `build_conversion_record` writes `model_type`, `detection.detection_arch = 'rf_detr_seg'`, `rfdetr_seg_size`, `mask_threshold`, `output_mode`, `top_k = num_queries` beside the detection fields (the three Seg keys are added to the params and, inside the DETR-family block of `build_conversion_record`, to the record only for `rf_detr_seg`, read with `params.get` — never as `… if arch == 'rf_detr_seg' else None` entries in the `:444-455` literal, which would move the exact ten-key YOLO params pin `tests/test_detector_conversion_request.py:63-67` against Requirement 7.9; the record's two `MODEL_TYPE_OBJECT_DETECTION` literals, `metadata.model_type` `:571` and `record.model_type` `:585`, become `MODEL_TYPE_FOR_ARCH[arch]`, the same value for `yolo` / `rf_detr` — C28); `build_conversion_job_request` sends the environment `DETECTION_ARCH=rf_detr_seg` (the record's arch, the unchanged wire — it passes `params['arch']` verbatim, `detector_conversion.py:503`), `RFDETR_SIZE=<rfdetr_seg_size>` (the detection key reused) and — **Seg only** — `TASK=segmentation` and `MASK_THRESHOLD`, so a `yolo` / `rf_detr` environment is byte-identical and the exact-env pin `tests/test_detector_conversion_request.py:211-215` holds (C26).

**Conversion job.** The job's gates at 43cd4ff1 reject a Seg job as sent (C26): `export_checkpoint.py:71` `ARCHES = ("yolo", "rf_detr")` → the `:203-205` fatal on `DETECTION_ARCH=rf_detr_seg`; `:216-219` `INPUT_BOUNDS[arch]` (`:84`, no Seg key) and `% INPUT_STEP` (`:83`, rejects 312 / 432 / 504 / 624); `:230-233` `RFDETR_SIZE` tied to `arch == "rf_detr"` and nano–large; `run()` dispatching on `cfg.arch` at `:1046-1049` (`verify_rfdetr_seg_contract` had no caller), `:1052` → `parity_inputs` (`:497`, ImageNet normalisation only for `== "rf_detr"`), `:1061` / `:965` `PARITY_TOLERANCE[cfg.arch]`; and `build_conversion_metadata`'s two-shape unpack and detection hints (`:985-1005`). So `read_config` → `ARCHES += 'rf_detr_seg'`, `INPUT_BOUNDS['rf_detr_seg'] = (240, 1152)`, the native-only input rule for Seg (mirror of `detector_conversion.py:391-398`), `RFDETR_SIZE` validated per arch table, `TASK` (`detection` default) cross-checked against the arch (FATAL on mismatch), `MASK_THRESHOLD` in (0, 1); `run()` gains a three-way contract dispatch and a family-wide `parity_inputs` (`is_detr_family`); `build_conversion_metadata` gains its Seg branch (tasks 7.2, 7.3, 7.6; parity of the mirrors by 1.5). `convert_rfdetr(checkpoint, cfg)` replaces the two fatals at `:854-864` with the task gate: `has_mask_head = any(k.startswith(_SEG_PREFIXES) for k in state)`; `cfg.task == 'segmentation' and not has_mask_head` → `fatal("the import recorded an RF-DETR-Seg checkpoint but the state dict has no segmentation head")`; the reverse → `fatal("checkpoint carries a segmentation head; the import recorded a detection checkpoint")`; `RFDETRSegPreview` and `RFDETRKeypoint*` stay fatal by name (Req 7.4). Size candidates come from `RFDETR_SEG_SIZE_CLASSES` for the Seg task (same shell-signature matching, `:871-889`); right after `trainer.export_onnx` (`:904`) and before `onnx_summary` and the two runtimes, the Seg task calls `trainer_seg.stamp_static_output_dims(path)` (M-1, task 0.3: the raw export's `masks` output is declared with four symbolic dims, which `onnx_summary`'s protobuf read — `export_checkpoint.py:285-320`, graph-output `dim_value` / `dim_param` — would report as `None` and `verify_rfdetr_seg_contract`, `check_onnx_against_record` and `inspect_onnx_model` would reject; the stamp is the single writer that makes the contract's "static ints" true for every downstream reader, verified live on 1.30.0 and 1.16.3 with byte-identical outputs); the reference closure (`:909-914`) is changed in one line — `return [t.detach().cpu().numpy() for t in out[:3 if cfg.task == 'segmentation' else 2]]` — because after `reference_module.export()` (`:906-907`; `:904` is the `trainer.export_onnx` call) the module's forward is `forward_export`, which returns the tuple `(pred_boxes, pred_logits, pred_masks)` for a Seg model (rfdetr 1.10.1 `lwdetr.py:623-717`, `:712-713`; WQ-design-author-3, settled at source), the third tensor being the dense mask logits `(1, Q, R/4, R/4)` on the native grid; the dict branch at `:912-913` stays for detection and is never taken for Seg (DD17). The spike's item S2 confirms only the numerics on a live export (names and static dims as loaded by the Export_Image's own onnxruntime (1.30.0 at 43cd4ff1, `requirements.lock:38`), the Fleet_Floor_Runtime 1.16.3 (`ort-floor.lock:10`) and the trainer DLC's 1.19.2 (`requirements-rfdetr.txt:8`, the pin behind `read_onnx_io` / `verify_seg_outputs`) — C17). `verify_rfdetr_seg_contract(summary, network_input, num_classes, trainer_seg)` = `verify_input_contract` + float32 on all three outputs + `trainer_seg.verify_seg_outputs(...)` (Req 7.4), where `trainer_seg` is `import train_rfdetr_seg` (the Export_Image copies the new file, §14). `compare_rfdetr_parity(reference, got, tol, task)` adds the mask term (DD17): for each matched pair `(q_ref, q_got)` above `score_floor`, `iou = |A ∧ B| / |A ∨ B|` of `sigmoid(mask) > cfg.mask_threshold` on the native grid; `min_iou < tol['mask_iou']` → fatal quoting the minimum and the pair; the measured minimum is written to the metadata as `parity.mask_iou_min` per runtime (Req 7.5). `PARITY_TOLERANCE['rf_detr_seg'] = {**PARITY_TOLERANCE['rf_detr'], 'mask_iou': 0.99}`. `compare_rfdetr_parity` pairs the reference and runtime arrays by shape and, for equal shapes, by position (`export_checkpoint.py:634-636`), which is correct because both lists are in graph order (the runtime outputs from `sess.run(None, …)` at `:422-424` / `:541` and the `forward_export` tuple, `:468`, `:551`; WQ-design-author-3), and the Seg term takes the only rank-4 array as the mask — so a three-class model (two `[1, Q, 4]` arrays) is paired correctly without names (C33 ii). Both runtimes (exporter's and `/opt/ort-floor` 1.16.3) run as today (`:434-469`), with the NaN/Inf, IR and opset checks, then the existing `normalize_ir_version` (clamp to `TARGET_IR = 8` only when the exporter stamped more than `FLEET_MAX_IR = 9`, `export_checkpoint.py:76-80, 324-336`; `verify_fleet_structure` fatals above 9, `:339-343`), which finalize re-checks at `detector_conversion.py:1080-1082` (`FLEET_MAX_IR = 9`, `:52`). The `≤ 10` cap (`_common.cap_onnx_ir_version`, `EDGE_MAX_ONNX_IR_VERSION = 10`, `_common.py:374-397`) belongs to the training entry point only (§4, Req 4.5) and is not applied by the conversion job. `build_conversion_metadata` writes the Seg keys (§Data contracts). `/opt/ml/model` holds exactly `model.onnx` and `training_metadata.json`; `check_onnx_against_record` / `check_metadata_against_record` validate three outputs with `[1,Q,4]`, `[1,Q,C+1]`, `[1,Q,R/4,R/4]` against the record's `top_k`, `num_classes`, `network_input` (Req 7.6). `check_onnx_against_record` (`detector_conversion.py:1076-1143`): for `rf_detr_seg` the rank test at `:1114-1117` is per output `(3, 3, 4)` (today `len(o['shape']) != 3` for **every** output rejects the rank-4 mask before any Seg logic runs) and the non-YOLO `else` at `:1134-1142` — which picks `logits` positionally as `next(s for s in shapes if s is not boxes)` and would take the mask whenever it precedes `labels` — picks `boxes` as a rank-3 shape with `s[2] == 4`, `logits` as the other rank-3 shape and `masks` as the rank-4 shape, requiring `boxes[1] == logits[1] == masks[1]`, `logits[2] == num_classes + 1` and `masks[2] == masks[3] == network_input // RFDETR_SEG_MASK_DOWNSAMPLE`, `summary['top_k'] = boxes[1]`; when both rank-3 shapes end in 4 (`num_classes + 1 == 4`) `boxes` / `logits` are settled by `summary['output_names']` (`:1123`; `dets` / `labels` — the names `verify_seg_outputs` already required for that case), falling back to output order as the detection `else` does, the two shapes being equal; the `rf_detr` path through the same `else` is byte-identical (C30, amended by tasks review pass 10: the pass-9 "`logits` = the rank-3 shape with `s[2] != 4`" pick is withdrawn — it rejected every three-class Seg model at finalize). `check_metadata_against_record` compares `sorted()` shape lists and `top_k` — order-free, no identification of its own. The sidecar and `metadata.fine_tunable = {arch: 'rf_detr_seg', kind: 'rfdetr_checkpoint', checkpoint_s3, class_names, num_classes}` are written exactly as for detection, and the legacy `export_format = 'pytorch'` path writes `arch` from the probe (`'rf_detr_seg'` for a Seg task, Req 7.7). Export_Image pins stay unless the spike proves otherwise (Req 7.8, §14).

Tests: the three rewritten cases of Req 7.2 (`test_detector_conversion_assessment.py:174`, `test_export_checkpoint_static.py:465, 478-480`) assert acceptance for `RFDETRSegSmall` and the `segmentation_head.*` fixture, and rejection for keypoint/legacy/preview; new cases for the task gate both ways, the native-only Seg rule (312/432/504/624 accepted, 320 rejected for every size, backend and `detectorConversion.ts`), `output_mode`/`mask_threshold`/`iou_threshold` validation, the three-output contract and parity mask term with synthetic reference/got arrays, the record and metadata keys, and the probe fixture; `test_model_converter_checkpoint_conversion.py` (YOLO-seg rejection) passes unmodified (Req 7.9). The **UI half** of this requirement — the Smart Import checkpoint form for a Seg `.pth` (`conversionLocksFor`, the locked tile, the request body) — is specified in §8 "Seg checkpoint locks" and tested there (`SmartImport.seg.test.tsx`, `detectorConversion.test.ts`); Req 18.2(c) runs it through the deployed UI.

### 8. Smart Import hardening: `model_converter.py`, `model_import.py`

Today (`model_converter.py`): `inspect_onnx_model` `n_out >= 3` branch (`:652-661`), `MODEL_TYPES` (`:128-145`), `generate_dda_package` Seg branch (`:878`, `:957`, `:967`, `:993-1015`), `convert_model` defaults (`:1146-1148`) and its stub `model_info` for ONNX (`:1226-1227`); `model_import.py` mapping (`:1370-1375`), `validate_mochi_json` taking `model_type` from `stages[0].type` (`:896-898`). D2c, O3 b, O4 b, O6 a, O9 a, O15 a; Req 8; DD6, DD7, DD15.

**Inspect.** The `n_out >= 3` branch requires exactly three outputs and the Seg_Output_Contract shapes: one rank-3 ending in 4, one rank-3 ending in `S`, one rank-4 `[1, Q, H, W]` sharing `Q` → `suggested_type = 'instance_segmentation'`, `detection_arch = 'rf_detr_seg'`, `num_classes = S - 1` (the trailing background slot; the UI may override to `S` for a foreign graph), `num_queries = Q`, `mask_grid = [H, W]`, `network_input` from the input tensor, `output_names` = the graph outputs' names in order (`_pb_all(graph, 12)` already yields them, `:621`; the inspector keeps `(name, dims)` pairs instead of dropping the names), hint `RF-DETR-Seg instance segmentation: boxes + logits + masks`. When `S == 4` the names decide boxes vs logits; with no names the inspector reports `unknown` with the ambiguity hint. **Foreign unstamped export (M-1, task 0.3):** a raw rfdetr 1.10.1 Seg export that never went through DDA's `stamp_static_output_dims` declares its rank-4 output with symbolic dims (`_pb_all` yields `None` for them); when the other two outputs match the contract and share `Q`, the inspector accepts a rank-4 output whose non-batch dims are `None` (or whose `Q` dim equals the shared `Q`) as `masks`, reports `mask_grid = [network_input // 4, network_input // 4]` (the `mask_downsample_ratio` of every Seg size) and appends `mask dims declared symbolically by the exporter; grid inferred as network_input / 4` to the hint; a rank-4 output with static dims that contradict `Q` or the inferred grid is still `unknown`. Three outputs that do not match → `suggested_type = 'unknown'` with a hint naming the shapes (Req 8.1). `MODEL_TYPES['instance_segmentation'] = "RF-DETR-Seg instance segmentation [1,Q,4] + [1,Q,C+1] + [1,Q,H,W]"`; the `segmentation` description is unchanged. `inspect_model_endpoint` adds `disabled_reason` when the Seg_Switch is OFF (DD7).

**Convert.** `convert_model` parses `output_mode` (default instance), `mask_threshold` (default `DEFAULT_MASK_THRESHOLD`) and, for `model_type == instance_segmentation`, `score_threshold` defaulting to `DEFAULT_SCORE_THRESHOLD['rf_detr_seg']` (0.5) instead of the generic 0.25 (`:1146`; the generic default is kept for every other type, Req 8.2). Order of checks for the two affected model types, placed right after the existing `MODEL_TYPES` check (`:1153-1156`): (1) `instance_segmentation` and the Seg_Switch OFF → 403; (2) the legacy alias: `model_type == 'segmentation' and detection_arch == 'rf_detr' and export_format == 'onnx'` → rewritten to `model_type = 'instance_segmentation'`, `detection_arch = 'rf_detr_seg'`, `output_mode = 'semantic'`, `deprecation` note queued for the response, then the switch check applies to it as to any Seg request (Req 8.5, 15.2); (3) `instance_segmentation` with `iou_threshold` in the body → 400 `'iou_threshold' does not apply to RF-DETR-Seg` (Req 8.3); (4) `instance_segmentation` with `detection_arch` other than `rf_detr_seg`/absent → 400. **Seg checkpoint locks (`utils/detectorConversion.ts`, `SmartImport.tsx`; Req 7 user story, 7.3, 18.2c).** The checkpoint form is driven by `conversionLocksFor(assessment)` (`detectorConversion.ts:265-306`), which today returns `null` unless `assessment.arch === 'yolo' || assessment.arch === 'rf_detr'` (`:267-268`); with `arch = 'rf_detr_seg'` from §7 the form would mark every Seg checkpoint `onnxBlocked` (`SmartImport.tsx:261-263`), fall back to `exportFormat = 'pytorch'` (`:371-372`) and say "ONNX is unavailable for this checkpoint" (`:885`), so no Seg conversion could start from the UI. [RFDETR_SEG_SIZES[size]] : RFDETR_SEG_NATIVE_INPUTS` (the six natives), `networkInput` = the trained resolution when it is in `allowedInputs`, else the single native, else `null` (the user must choose — the RF-DETR rule at `:282-285`), `inputBounds = {...INPUT_BOUNDS.rf_detr_seg, step: 1}` (no step; `networkInputProblem` (`:331-346`) takes the `allowedInputs` branch for Seg and its message names "RF-DETR-Seg <size> converts only at its native <n>px"), `rfdetrSize: null`, `rfdetrSegSize`, `scoreThreshold: DEFAULT_SCORE_THRESHOLD.rf_detr_seg`, `maskThreshold: DEFAULT_MASK_THRESHOLD`, `iouThreshold: null`, `preserveAspect: false`, geometry from `CONVERSION_GEOMETRY.rf_detr_seg`; `checkpointFamilyLabel` (`:191-197`) appends the size for `isRfDetrSegSize(assessment.rfdetr_seg_size)` too ("RF-DETR-Seg (small)"). In the page: the locked pre-fill (`:355-369`) also sets `maskThreshold` and `outputMode = 'instance'`; the tile rule at `:875` disables the three other tiles when `locks.modelType === 'instance_segmentation'` (so `SmartImport.checkpoint.test.tsx:292-294` stays as is for a detection checkpoint); the lock texts become `locks.modelType === 'instance_segmentation' ? 'Locked to Instance Segmentation: the checkpoint is an RF-DETR-Seg model.' : 'Locked to Object Detection: the checkpoint is a detector.'` (`:847`) and `Locked to RF-DETR-Seg` (`:918`, which renders only for `object_detection` today and is shown for the Seg lock too, read-only); the locked form (`:983-1070`) shows the mask-threshold field and the Output control (`instance` | `semantic overlay`) when `locks.modelType === 'instance_segmentation'` and hides the IoU field (`:1055-1068`, already `locks.arch === 'yolo'`-only); `handleConvertCheckpoint` (`:426-462`) validates `thresholdProblem('Mask threshold', maskThreshold)` for Seg and sends `model_type: conv.modelType`, `detection_arch: conv.arch`, `mask_threshold` and `output_mode` for Seg, `iou_threshold` only for YOLO as today (`:457`), the rest of the body unchanged.
