### 9. Device decoder: `RfDetrSegPostProcessor`

Today: `RfDetrSemanticSegmentationPostProcessor` (`src/backend/lyra_science_processing_utils/model_processors/rf_detr_semantic_segmentation_postprocessor.py`: config `:66-77`, decode `:79-119`, `_split_outputs :122-157`), `RfDetrDetectionPostProcessor` (flattened top-k `:102-134`, `_split_outputs :156-196`, `_make_result :200-219`), `ObjectDetectionResult` (`utils/object_detection_result.py:33-56`), `AnomalyResult` (`utils/anomaly_result.py:72-86`), `SingleStageModelGraph.predict` (`model_graphs/single_stage_model_graph.py:100-130`), `MODEL_NAME_MAP` (`model_processors/__init__.py:52-65`), `ModelGraphFactory` (`model_graph_factory.py:66-80`). D1, D5, O3 b, O6 a, O15 a, O16 a; Req 9; DD2, DD3, DD5, DD11, DD18.

New module `model_processors/rf_detr_seg_postprocessor.py`:

```python
MODE_INSTANCE, MODE_SEMANTIC = "instance", "semantic"
STAGE_TYPE_INSTANCE, STAGE_TYPE_SEMANTIC = "rf_detr_instance_segmentation", "rf_detr_semantic_segmentation"
DEFAULT_SCORE_THRESHOLD = DEFAULT_MASK_THRESHOLD = 0.5
DEFAULT_TOP_K_FOR_INPUT = {312: 100, 384: 100, 432: 200, 504: 200, 624: 300, 768: 300}   # size → queries, keyed by network_input
DEFAULT_NETWORK_INPUT = 312

class RfDetrSegPostProcessor(InferencePostProcessor):
    def __init__(self, config):                      # config = the stage dict (type, image_width, detection{...})
        self.mode = MODE_INSTANCE if config.get("type") == STAGE_TYPE_INSTANCE else MODE_SEMANTIC
        detection = config.get("detection", {})
        declared = detection.get("output_mode")      # checked against the stage type: contradiction → LOG.error once, stage type wins
        self.score_threshold, self.mask_threshold = …(0.5 defaults)
        self.num_classes = detection.get("num_classes")           # None → all slots (foreign graph)
        self.network_input = …                                    # detection.network_input or image_width or 312
        self.top_k = int(detection.get("top_k") or DEFAULT_TOP_K_FOR_INPUT.get(self.network_input, 300))
        self.output_names = detection.get("output_names")         # graph order, optional (DD18)
        self.class_names = detection.get("class_names") or None

    def __call__(self, model_output, *args, src_img_size=None, **kwargs):
        boxes, logits, masks = self._split_outputs(model_output)  # None triple → empty result for the mode, LOG.warning once per instance
        instances = self._select(boxes, logits, masks, out_w, out_h)   # List[InstanceSegmentationResult], score descending, ≤ top_k
        if self.mode == MODE_INSTANCE:
            return instances                                             # the graph wraps the list; nothing else is built
        top = instances[0].confidence if instances else 0.0
        return AnomalyResult(score=top, mask=paint_composite(instances, out_h, out_w))
```

`_select` builds `InstanceSegmentationResult` objects **directly** (no intermediate record type), so the semantic branch and the Triton base model call `paint_composite` with the same argument type — one function, one record shape (Req 9.5, 10.1).

`_split_outputs` identifies the three tensors by shape in any order after squeezing a leading batch dim of 1 (`[Q,4]`, `[Q,S]`, `[Q,Hm,Wm]`); fewer than three usable tensors or a `Q` mismatch → `(None, None, None)` with one `LOG.warning` per processor instance (a `_warned` flag), never a raise (Req 9.2). When the two rank-2 tensors both have last dim 4 (`S == 4`): if `self.output_names` names `dets`/`labels`, their positions in the runner's list decide (the runner returns `session.run(None, …)` in graph order, `inference_runtimes.py:386-389`); else the list order is taken as `[boxes, logits]` with a one-time `LOG.warning` (DD18). The same tie-break is added to `RfDetrDetectionPostProcessor._split_outputs` by reading `detection.output_names` in its `__init__`.

`_select` (Req 9.3, 9.4): `scores = sigmoid(logits[:, :C])` with `C = min(num_classes, S)` when `num_classes` is set (a `num_classes > S` logs an error once and uses `S`; `num_classes == S` scores all slots — the foreign-graph case; `num_classes is None` scores all slots); flattened top-k over `Q × C` pairs with `k = min(top_k, Q × C)` exactly as `rf_detr_detection_postprocessor.py:118-134`; keep `score ≥ score_threshold`. For each kept pair, in descending score order: `prob = sigmoid(masks[q])` on the `Hm × Wm` grid, `cv2.resize(prob, (out_w, out_h), interpolation=cv2.INTER_LINEAR)` (bilinear, float32), `binary = resized > mask_threshold` (rfdetr's reference order, `postprocess.py:247-267`), `area = int(binary.sum())`; `area == 0` → dropped (a box without a mask); otherwise `packed = np.packbits(binary.ravel())` and the dense arrays are released before the next pair (DD3). Upsampling runs only for kept pairs; a query kept for two classes is upsampled twice (two instances, as the detection decoder yields two boxes). The box is `_cxcywh_to_xyxy(boxes[q]) × (out_w, out_h)` with the squash geometry of `_make_result` (`:200-219`). Each kept pair becomes `InstanceSegmentationResult(bounding_box, obj_class, confidence=score, threshold=self.score_threshold, class_index=c, mask_packed=packed, mask_shape=(out_h, out_w), mask_area=area)` there and then (`obj_class` = `class_names[c]` when names are configured, else `str(c)`, as `_make_result` does); the list is capped at `top_k` entries, ordered by score descending.

`paint_composite(instances: Sequence[InstanceSegmentationResult], out_h: int, out_w: int) -> np.uint8[H, W]` is a module-level pure function typed on the result class and reading only `.confidence`, `.class_index` and `.mask()` (the duck-typed protocol, stated once here): iterate the instances **ascending** by `.confidence`, `mask = inst.mask()` (`np.unpackbits(mask_packed, count=h*w).reshape(h, w).astype(bool)`), `index[mask] = min(class_index + 1, PALETTE_MAX_INDEX)` with the decoder-module constant `PALETTE_MAX_INDEX = 103` (pinned by a test to `len(DEFAULT_ANOMALY_MASK_PALETTE) - 1`; the decoder module keeps importing nothing from `lyra_anomalies_mask_utils`, which only the Triton base model imports, so the sandbox duplicate stays self-contained): the palette has **104** rows (`src/backend/lyra_anomalies_mask_utils/__init__.py:6-113`) and `convert_index_mask_to_color_mask` indexes `palette[: mask.max() + 1][mask]` (`:117-133`), so any index ≥ 104 would raise `IndexError` inside the Triton base model — a latent fault the semantic path already has today for a graph with more than 103 classes, closed here for both modes. The decoder logs once (`WARN: <n> classes exceed the 103-colour palette; classes ≥ 103 share the last colour in the Composite_Mask`) when `num_classes > 103`; the Instance_Results themselves are unaffected (their `class` index and `mask_rle` are exact), only the composite's colour saturates. The limit is documented in §24 of the device doc (§15) and tested with a 110-class synthetic decoder case (Req 9.7). Both modes and the Triton base model use this one function (Req 9.5, 10.1).

Instance results (Req 9.5, DD11): `utils/object_detection_result.py` gains

```python
class InstanceSegmentationResult(ObjectDetectionResult):
    def __init__(self, bounding_box, obj_class, confidence, threshold, class_index: int, mask_packed: np.ndarray, mask_shape: (h, w), mask_area: int)
    def mask(self) -> np.ndarray                      # dense bool, unpacked on demand (used by paint_composite in the base model)
    def serialize(self) -> Dict:                       # ObjectDetectionResult.serialize() + {"class_index": int, "mask_rle": <Canonical_RLE str>, "mask_area": int}
    @classmethod deserialize(cls, data)                # mask_rle → packed via mask_rle.decode (tests, export)
```

`serialize()` encodes with `utils/mask_rle.encode(mask)` (DD5). `obj_class` keeps the detection decoder's meaning (label when `class_names` is present, else the index string), so `resolve_class_label` in the base model (`lfv_model_template.py:335`) and the marshal behave as for detection. `SingleStageModelGraph.predict` needs no change: a list whose first element `isinstance(..., ObjectDetectionResult)` is wrapped as detections (`:116-118`), an `AnomalyResult` as anomaly (`:121-128`). In instance mode the decoder returns `[]` for zero kept instances, which the graph turns into `InferenceData()` and the base model into the zero-object sentinel (Req 10.4).

Semantic mode returns `AnomalyResult(score=max score or 0.0, mask=paint_composite(instances, out_h, out_w))` over the same `InstanceSegmentationResult` list — the D5 manifest (stage type `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation`, stage-level `detection` without `output_mode`/`top_k`/`output_names`) decodes in semantic mode with `top_k` from `DEFAULT_TOP_K_FOR_INPUT[312]` (Req 9.1). Compared with today's decode, the semantic output changes in two ways O3 b requires: bilinear instead of nearest resize (edge pixels) and flattened top-k instead of per-query argmax (a query can contribute two classes; the higher score paints last and wins). The eight existing tests are adjusted only where these two rules change an assertion, each adjustment commented (Req 9.7).

Registry: `MODEL_NAME_MAP["financier"] = ("rf_detr_semantic_segmentation", RfDetrSegPostProcessor)` and `MODEL_NAME_MAP["macaron"] = ("rf_detr_instance_segmentation", RfDetrSegPostProcessor)`; `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation_postprocessor.py` becomes the import shim of DD2. Duplicates under `edge-cv-portal/test-sandbox/dda_triton_resources/lyra_science_processing_utils/` — `model_processors/rf_detr_seg_postprocessor.py` (new), `rf_detr_semantic_segmentation_postprocessor.py`, `rf_detr_detection_postprocessor.py`, `__init__.py`, `utils/object_detection_result.py`, `utils/mask_rle.py` (new) — are byte-identical copies made in the same commit; `model_graphs/single_stage_model_graph.py` is not touched (Req 9.7, 17.5).

Tests (`test/backend-test/test_rf_detr_seg_postprocessor.py`, plus the adjusted eight): instance mode count/order/box geometry/`mask_rle` round trip/`mask_area`/mask threshold; flattened top-k with a query yielding two classes; slot handling (`C+1`, `C`, over-declared); shuffled/nameless tensors; the three-class tie-break via `output_names` and the list-order fallback warning; too-few outputs → `[]` / all-background; `top_k` cap; zero-foreground drop; the D5 legacy manifest; the `output_mode` contradiction log; `paint_composite` overlap precedence and the palette saturation (a 110-class manifest: composite index never exceeds 103, one WARN, `convert_index_mask_to_color_mask` on the result does not raise); `test_mask_rle.py` against the shared vector file (DD5); the three-class case for `RfDetrDetectionPostProcessor` (Req 17.2).

### 10. Device result contract: Triton base and marshal, readers, DB, run preview, HMI

Today: `__build_detection_tensors` (`src/backend/dda_triton/resources_for_copy/lfv_model_template.py:312-372`; empty `mask` at `:364`), `__build_anomaly_tensors` (`:374-420`, colour mask via `convert_index_mask_to_color_mask` `:394`), the `setdefault` merge (`:528-535`); the marshal's `_is_detection_list` (`marshal_for_capture_template.py:161-170`), `_generate_detection_overlay` (`:172-254`), `_generate_capture_meta_data` (`:269-526`: colour map `:319-342`, detections block `:437-492`), `_has_anomaly_mask` (`:548-549`), `execute` (`:551-660`: detection branch with empty `mask` `:635-645`); readers `inference_results_utils.save_image_object` (`:69-140`), `convert_inference_res_to_save_in_db` (`:330-361`), `InferenceResult` ORM (`dao/sqlite_db/models.py:117-146`), schema + plain class (`model/inference_result.py:31-108`), `InferenceResultHistoryModel` (`data_models/common.py:139-162`), endpoints (`endpoints/inference_result.py:117-148`), `workflow_engine/detections.py` (`read_detections :118`, `build_detection_list :254`), `run_artifacts.py` (`read_mask_overlay :234-253`, `_mask_background_from_outputs :309-333`), `api.py` overlay endpoint (`:481-499`), HMI (`src/frontend/src/components/deployed-workflow/detections.ts:62-75`, `src/frontend/src/components/deployed-workflow/results/RunResults.tsx:341-368`, `src/frontend/src/components/live-result/LiveResultCard.tsx:60-70` and `src/frontend/src/components/result-history/types.ts:32-63` — the last two live directly under `components/`, not under `deployed-workflow/`). D1, O1 a, O2 b; Req 10; DD3, DD4, DD11–DD14.

**Base model (`lfv_model_template.py`).** `__build_detection_tensors` keeps its shape and adds three things. (1) Each entry is `d.serialize()` + `class_label` as today; `InstanceSegmentationResult.serialize()` already contributes `class_index`, `mask_rle`, `mask_area` (DD11). (2) The byte ceiling (DD4): `payload_json = json.dumps(payload)`; while `len(payload_json) > DETECTION_LIST_MAX_BYTES` (4 MiB) and some entry still has `mask_rle`, pop `mask_rle` from the lowest-confidence such entry, set `entry["mask_truncated"] = True`, re-dump; a `LOG.warning` once per capture with the count dropped. (3) The composite: `instances = [d for d in detections if isinstance(d, InstanceSegmentationResult)]`; if `instances and self.__model_supports_anomaly_localization` (non-empty `pixel_level_classes`, `:196-197`), `index = paint_composite(instances, h, w)` with `(h, w) = input_np.shape[:2]` (the source frame the graph received; the decoder produced masks at `src_img_size`, which is that frame), `mask_tensor = convert_index_mask_to_color_mask(index)` (`:394`); otherwise the empty mask as today (`:364`). YOLO/RF-DETR detections have no `InstanceSegmentationResult`, so their tensors are byte-identical (Req 10.1). Zero instances: `detections == []` → the sentinel at `:343-357`, empty mask, `output = 0` (Req 10.4).

**Marshal (`marshal_for_capture_template.py`).** Imports: `DEFAULT_ANOMALY_MASK_PALETTE` and `hex_color_string` from `lyra_anomalies_mask_utils` inside the existing `try/except ImportError` block (`:49-61`), setting `_MASK_UTILS = False` on failure (Req 10.1). New pure helpers: `_has_instance_masks(anomalies) -> bool` (`_is_detection_list(anomalies) and any(isinstance(e.get("mask_rle"), str) for e in anomalies)`); `_instance_color_map(mask_rgb, entries, label_key) -> dict` (DD12: background share from palette row 0 over the whole mask, one entry per `class_index` present in `entries` with the share of pixels equal to `DEFAULT_ANOMALY_MASK_PALETTE[class_index + 1]`, labels `background` / `class_label`, hex lower-cased via `hex_color_string(row.tolist()).lower()`, keys `"0"`, `"1"`, … in class order); `_fill_instance_masks(overlay, entries, alpha=0.4)` decoding each `mask_rle` with a local vectorised decoder (the marshal cannot import `lyra_science_processing_utils.utils.mask_rle` unguarded; the decoder is ~10 lines of numpy duplicated here on purpose, pinned by the same vector file) and blending the class colour (palette row `class_index + 1`) under the boxes `_generate_detection_overlay` draws (`:172-254`). `_generate_capture_meta_data`: `is_detection` as today; when `is_detection and self._has_anomaly_mask(inference_mask, input_image)` the mask file path and `anomaly-mask-ref-detected` are written as for anomaly (`:319-325`), and `internal-color-map` comes from `_instance_color_map(inference_mask, inference_anomalies, "name")` instead of the `anomalies[i]["name"]` loop (`:329-334`, which would `KeyError` on a Detection_List); `type`, `creation-date`, `human-annotated`, `job-name` as for anomaly (`:335-338`). In the detections block (`:447-469`) each `det_map[str(idx)]` entry copies `mask_rle`, `mask_area`, `mask_truncated` when present; `class_index` prefers `det.get("class_index")` (DD11); `detection_data = {"detections": det_map}` gains `"detections_truncated": True` when any entry had `mask_truncated`, and `"mask_color_map": _instance_color_map(inference_mask, inference_anomalies, "class-name")` when a mask was written and `_MASK_UTILS` (Req 10.3, 10.1); the sidecar carries the same `detection_data` (`:479-483`). On `_MASK_UTILS` false: `mask.png` is still written, `internal-color-map` and `mask_color_map` are omitted, one `log.warning`. `execute`'s detection branch (`:635-645`): overlay = `_generate_detection_overlay` then `_fill_instance_masks` when `_has_instance_masks`; `mask_tensor` = the encoded `inference_mask` when `_has_anomaly_mask(...)` else the empty array as today. Semantic mode never enters these branches (`_is_detection_list` is false) and produces today's record (Req 10.7).

**Readers (Req 10.5).** (a) Live result — `save_image_object`'s detection branch (`inference_results_utils.py:74-99`) keeps `detections` as the **map** (`:97`) with the new per-entry keys passing through, copies `detections_truncated` **only when the block carries it** (`if 'detections_truncated' in detections_block: infer_res['detections_truncated'] = bool(...)`) — the marshal writes the marker only when an entry lost its `mask_rle` (data contract, Capture record), so a YOLO / RF-DETR capture keeps exactly today's six-key dict (`inference_results_utils.py:89-99`) and the key-for-key pin `test/backend-test/utils/test_property_detection_result_preservation.py:742-782` (`test_detection_branch_output_is_unchanged`) holds unmodified (Req 17.1; C21) — and when `get_mask_base64_image(output_list)` (`:247`) returns a mask: `mask_image`, `mask_background = convert_hex_color_to_rgb(mask_color_map["0"])` and `anomalies = {k: v for k, v in mask_color_map.items() if k != "0"}` when `detections_block.get("mask_color_map")` is present, else `mask_image` alone. (b) Result history — `convert_inference_res_to_save_in_db` (`:330-361`) adds `db_inference_res["detections"] = [det_map[k] for k in sorted(det_map, key=int)]` when `inference_res["inferenceResult"].get("detections")` is a non-empty map (list form, DD13); the four sites: ORM `detections = Column(JSON)` + Alembic revision (`down_revision = '37b6e36641e7'`), `InferenceResultSchema.detections = fields.List(fields.Dict(), required=False)` + `InferenceResult.__init__(..., detections=None)` attribute, `InferenceResultHistoryModel.detections: Optional[List[dict]] = None`, single-row GET unchanged; `EditInferenceResult` unchanged. (c) Run preview — `run_artifacts.read_mask_overlay` returns `{maskImage, maskBackground, instances, detectionsTruncated}` (DD14): `instances` = `_instances_from_sidecar_or_record(output_dir, capture_id)` using `detections.read_detections` (`:118-151`, sidecar first, record second; entries kept verbatim incl. `mask_rle`) converted to the list form, or `None`; `_mask_background_from_outputs` (`:309-333`) tries `label["anomalies"]["0"]` then `label["mask_color_map"]["0"]`. The `/overlay` endpoint (`api.py:481-499`) returns the extended **four-key** dict — `instances` and `detectionsTruncated` are `null` for non-instance runs — so the exact-body pin `test/backend-test/workflow_engine/test_workflow_run_results_api.py:308` is **extended** to the four keys in the same commit (Req 17.3; C21). (d) Run metadata — `build_detection_list` / `_normalize_entry` (`detections.py:254-368`) pass `mask_area` through when present and never copy `mask_rle` (telemetry stays small); `merge_detections` callers in `pipeline_executor.py:2415` and `python_bridge.py:1762` are untouched (so the CSI golden on `pipeline_executor.py` does not move, Req 17.3); `output_bindings.py:72` and conditions see the same `metadata.detections` entries plus `mask_area`. One more in-device reader of the raw Detection_List is unchanged by construction: the debug-bundle recorder (`src/backend/workflow_engine/telemetry/debug/recorder.py:365`) calls `detections_module.read_detections(rr.output_dir, rr.capture_id)` directly when the run result carries no list and projects each entry through `_detection_item` (`:384-401`), which copies only `label`, `confidence`, `box` and `id` — `mask_rle` and `mask_area` never reach the debug bundle, so its size and schema are unaffected and no task touches it.

**HMI (Req 10.6).** `src/frontend/src/components/deployed-workflow/instanceDetections.ts` (DD14; placed beside `detections.ts` because HMI helpers live next to the components that use them — `src/frontend/src` holds only `api/`, `components/`, `config/`, `styles/`, `static/` and top-level files at 43cd4ff1, no `utils/`): `normalizeDetectionEntries(source: Record<string, Entry> | Entry[] | null): Entry[]` (map → entries in ascending key order; list → as is), `decodeMaskRle(rle: string, width: number, height: number): Uint8Array` (the column-major decoder, same vectors as DD5), `maskToImageData(bits, w, h, rgba)`. `components/deployed-workflow/results/InstanceMaskLayer.tsx` draws the entries' masks on a `<canvas>` sized to the rendered image and the boxes above it, with a `masks + boxes | boxes` toggle (`SegmentedControl`), and shows `mask_area` in `DetectedObjectsTable` as an extra column when any entry carries it — carried by `RunDetection.maskArea?: number` and a `runDetections` copy of a finite numeric `mask_area` set only when present (beside the `id` copy, `detections.ts:100-102`); `DetectedObjectsTable` builds the `Mask area (px)` column as a conditional column array like `violationColumn` (`:84-94`), inserted after `Bounding box (px)` (`:133-137`) and present only when some row has `maskArea` (C20: at 43cd4ff1 `RunDetection` (`:33-41`) is `{id?, label, confidence, box?}` and `runDetections` (`:62-105`) copies only `label`, `confidence`, `box` and `id`, while the table's one feeder is `RunResults.tsx:345` `runDetections(metadataQuery.data)` — so the column had no carrier). Sources: RunResults fetches `getWorkflowExecutionOverlay(executionId)` (`api/WorkflowRegistrationAPI.ts:355-359`; the query already exists for `maskImage`) and renders the layer from `instances` when non-null, keeping `runDetections(metadata)` for the table; `src/frontend/src/components/live-result/LiveResultCard.tsx` renders from `inferenceResult.detections` (map form, `Object.values` today, `:70`); the result-history detail renders from the row's `detections` (list form). **What the instance live card shows:** today the card is a ternary — `isDetection` (`prediction === PredictionType.Detection`, `:72`) shows "Objects detected" from `detectionList`, otherwise "Anomaly labels" from `labelInfoList = Object.values(anomalies ?? [])` (`:67-108`) — so an instance result (which §10 (a) gives both `detections` and `anomalies` = the per-class mask coverage from `mask_color_map`) would show the objects list and silently hide the coverage. The card keeps the ternary (detection and anomaly results render exactly as today) and, when `isDetection && labelInfoList.length > 0`, adds a second `ValueWithLabel` "Mask coverage" under "Objects detected" listing each class's `total-percentage-area` with its colour swatch via the existing `AnomalyLabels` component (`live-result/AnomalyLabels.tsx`); a plain detection result has no `anomalies`, so nothing changes for it. The Jest case (`src/frontend` is a CRA app: `package.json` `"test": "react-scripts test"`, `react-scripts 5.0.1`, `@testing-library/react` + `@testing-library/jest-dom`; its 37 existing `*.test.ts(x)` files use Jest globals and none imports `vitest`) renders the card with an instance fixture and asserts both lists, and with a detection fixture and asserts "Mask coverage" is absent. `src/frontend/src/components/result-history/types.ts:32-37` `DetectionResult` (`{boundingBox, objectClass, confidence}`, camelCase) has **no consumer** outside its definition at 43cd4ff1 (`grep -rn "DetectionResult\b" src/frontend/src` finds only `types.ts`); it is replaced by the entry shape `{class_index: string; class_label: string; bounding_box: number[]; confidence: number; mask_rle?: string; mask_area?: number; mask_truncated?: boolean}` that `InstanceMaskLayer` and the history detail use — a dead-type replacement, no caller to migrate. The existing detection-only and anomaly-only renderings are unchanged (their Jest suites pass unmodified; the HMI suite runs as `CI=true npm test -- --watchAll=false` from `src/frontend`, the command the precedent that created it uses, `.kiro/specs/edge-workflow-run-ux/tasks.md:7, 77`). New HMI tests are `*.test.ts(x)` files beside their component, written with Jest globals and `@testing-library/react`, as the 37 existing ones are.

**Edge MCP (Req 13.3).** One rule for **every** edge tool whose result carries the Detection_List, with no exception: boxes and `mask_area` are returned, `mask_rle` is dropped, the mask image (`maskImage` / `mask_image`) stays dropped or extracted exactly as today. The rule has to be exceptionless because of the result cap: `shaping.MAX_RESULT_BYTES = 200_000` (`src/backend/mcp_edge/child/shaping.py:56`); for an **object-shaped** result `bound` does not truncate but returns `ToolFailure('result_too_large', …)` when the encoded body exceeds it (`:334-340`), and `drop_fields` runs before `bound` in `shape_json` (`:395-423`), so dropping is the only way an instance row with a few 1080p masks (≈ 50 KB each as text, DD4) can be returned at all. Five tools carry the list after this spec: (1) `edge_results_list.drop_fields = ('results[].maskImage', 'results[].detections[].mask_rle')` (`src/backend/mcp_edge/child/catalog.py:845`; the DB rows hold the **list** form, DD13, so the existing `[]` fan-out works); (2) `edge_result_get` (`:846-855`, object-shaped, `image_field='maskImage'`) gains `drop_fields=('detections[].mask_rle',)` — its Composite_Mask still reaches the caller as the extracted `maskImage` image, boxes and `mask_area` stay in the body (this corrects Requirement 13.3's "`edge_result_get` returns the full entries"; see "Requirements corrections", C9); (3) `edge_execution_overlay_get` (`:759-761`, built by `_execution_read(..., image_field='maskImage')`, which passes `**extra` through to `ToolSpec`, `:456-465`) gains `drop_fields=('instances[].mask_rle',)` for the extended `/overlay` dict (its `maskImage` unchanged); (4) `edge_workflow_images_list` (`:548-560`, handler `endpoints.workflow.list_images`) and (5) `edge_workflow_run` (`:606-628`, handler `run_inference_for_stream`) return the **live** `inferenceResult` dict that `save_image_object` builds — whose `detections` is the **map** form `{"0": {...}, "1": {...}}` (`inference_results_utils.py:97`; §10 (a)) — and already drop `images[].inferenceResult.mask_image` / `inferenceResult.mask_image`. `shaping.resolve_path` fans `[]` over lists only (`shaping.py:136-160`), so a path cannot reach the map's values today; the design therefore adds a **dict-wildcard segment `{}`** to the path grammar, mirroring `[]`: `_PATH_RE` (`shaping.py:64`) accepts `name{}` beside `name[]`; `_parse_path` returns `(name, mode)` with `mode ∈ {None, 'list', 'dict'}`; `resolve_path` fans a `dict` step over `node[name].values()` (at the leaf it yields `(node[name], key)` for every key, so `drop_fields` deletes by key — its sort key `t[1] if isinstance(t[1], int) else 0` already tolerates string keys, `:168-171`) and yields nothing when the value is not a dict; the two path consumers `drop_fields` and `extract_image` take the new mode through `resolve_path` unchanged, and `drop_oversized_images`, `scrub_paths` and `bound` do not use paths (the edge module defines `_parse_path`, `resolve_path`, `drop_fields`, `extract_image`, `drop_oversized_images`, `scrub_paths`, `bound`, `shape_json`, `shape`, `shaping.py:129-440`; `check_binding` is a Portal-server function, `mcp_portal/shaping.py:92`, not an edge one). Then `edge_workflow_images_list.drop_fields += ('images[].inferenceResult.detections{}.mask_rle',)` and `edge_workflow_run.drop_fields += ('inferenceResult.detections{}.mask_rle',)`. Pins this moves (same commit): `DOTTED_PATH` at `test/backend-test/mcp_edge/test_property_child_catalog.py:40` (asserted for every `drop_fields` path at `:147`) gains the `{}` alternative, and the helpers `_fill` / `_resolves` of `test_property_child_shaping.py:129-166` learn `{}` (`_fill` builds `{'0': make(), '1': make()}` for a dict step; `_resolves` walks `value.values()`), so `test_drop_fields_resolve_to_nothing_and_are_listed_iff_present` (`:183`) keeps covering every path of every tool. Pins that hold: `len(CATALOG) == 73` / 47 read-only (`test_property_child_catalog.py:184`) and the 47 Phase-1 tools (`test_child_protocol.py:99`) — no tool is added; the object-result cap itself (`MAX_RESULT_BYTES = 200_000`, `shaping.py:56`) is not raised. Rejected: a per-tool post-shape hook (`ToolSpec` has none, `catalog.py:207-253`, and a second mechanism beside `drop_fields` would not be covered by the property test) and converting the live dict's `detections` to a list for the HTTP endpoints (the HMI live card reads the map with `Object.values`, Req 10.5a). `docs/edge-mcp-server.md:205` gets one sentence on the `{}` segment (§15).

**Duplicates (Req 10.8, 17.5).** `lfv_model_template.py`, `marshal_for_capture_template.py` and `inference_runtimes.py` under `edge-cv-portal/test-sandbox/dda_triton_resources/` receive the same hunks in the same commit; the pre-existing drift is not reconciled. The hunk list the identity test asserts in both copies (string anchors): base model — `DETECTION_LIST_MAX_BYTES`, `mask_truncated`, `paint_composite(`, `InstanceSegmentationResult`; marshal — `_has_instance_masks`, `_instance_color_map`, `_fill_instance_masks`, `mask_color_map`, `detections_truncated`; `inference_runtimes.py` has no Seg hunk (the runner is unchanged) and is listed as "no change expected". The `lyra_science_processing_utils` and `lyra_anomalies_mask_utils` trees are asserted byte-identical for every file this spec touches (§9).

**Tests (Req 10.8).** `test/backend-test/test_lfv_model_template_instances.py` and `test_marshal_instances.py` with `pb_utils` stubbed as the existing Triton template tests do: both modes, zero instances (sentinel, no `mask.png`), the ceiling (entries lose `mask_rle` lowest-confidence first, `detections_truncated` written), the sidecar, the colour map and `mask_color_map`, the `_MASK_UTILS` fallback, YOLO entries byte-identical; `test_inference_results_utils_instances.py` (live dict, DB row list form, schema + plain class, migration up/down on a scratch SQLite, `GET /workflows/{id}/results` and `/results/{capture_id}` return the list); `test_run_artifacts_instances.py`; `test_detections_mask_area.py`; edge MCP — `test/backend-test/mcp_edge/test_child_shaping_dict_wildcard.py` (new: `resolve_path` over `a{}.b`, nested `x[].y{}.z`, a non-dict value yields nothing, `drop_fields` records the path iff present) plus the five tools' drop rules exercised through `shaping.shape_json` on fixtures carrying `mask_rle` in list and map form (all five drop it; `edge_result_get` on a stored row with 50 instance masks of ~50 KB each yields a `Shaped` result with boxes, `mask_area` and the extracted `maskImage`, never `result_too_large`), and the existing property tests pass with the `{}`-aware helpers; HMI Jest (`CI=true npm test -- --watchAll=false --testPathPattern=<pattern>` from `src/frontend`) for `instanceDetections.ts`, `InstanceMaskLayer`, RunResults/LiveResultCard/result-history with instance fixtures (incl. the instance card's "Mask coverage" sub-list).

### 11. Portal results: `captures.py`, `ResultsViewer.tsx`, inference-results export

Today: `captures.py` `list_captures` (`edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/captures.py:137-231`: query parameters `:155-159`, prefix scoping `:189-191`, paginator and sequential parse `:203-220`, response `:222-228`), `_parse_capture` (`:234-277`), `_extract_inference_data` (`:300-344`), presigned `overlay_url`/`mask_url` (`:265-267`), the per-capture dict (`:268-276`); `ResultsViewer.tsx` types (`:53-66`), detection presentation with SVG boxes and the overlay toggle (`:115-200`), anomaly composite from `mask_url` (`:239-265`), `isDetection` gate (`:290`); the device export `generate_smgt_format_manifest` (`src/backend/utils/inference_results_utils.py:363-384`) called by `src/backend/endpoints/download_file.py:360` (route `GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/results/export`, `:311-312`). D1, O1 a, O2 b, O8 a; Req 11; DD20.

**`captures.py` (Req 11.1).** `_extract_inference_data(metadata, include_masks=False)` **keeps its 3-tuple return** `(inference_result_type, detection_count, detections)` (`captures.py:300-344`; unpacked as three values at `:259` and by five direct tests, `tests/test_captures.py:207, 227, 240, 249, 264`, which therefore pass unmodified): the detections normaliser `_normalize_detections` (`:347`) keeps its four keys and passes through `mask_area` and `mask_truncated` when present, and `mask_rle` **only on a per-capture detail request** (`key` present **and** `include_masks` true, below); a page request never carries `mask_rle`, whatever `include_masks` says, so the page's size bound is structural rather than a client convention. The two new per-capture flags come from a sibling `_capture_mask_flags(block: Optional[Dict]) -> Tuple[bool, bool]` returning `(detections_truncated, has_masks)` from the **decoded** detections block: `detections_truncated = bool(block.get("detections_truncated"))`; `has_masks = any("mask_rle" in d or d.get("mask_truncated") for d in block["detections"].values())` is computed from the raw block, so it is true whenever any entry carried a mask on the device — whether the RLE is returned in this response, withheld on a page request, or dropped at the DD4 ceiling (an all-truncated capture, where every entry has `mask_truncated: true` and `mask_area` but no `mask_rle`, still reports `has_masks = true`, so the viewer keeps its instance presentation and its truncated-instances note); `(False, False)` for `None` (anomaly or empty records). The base64 block decode (`:334-342` today: `_get_aux_output` → `base64.b64decode` → `json.loads`, failure printed and swallowed) is factored into `_decode_detections_block(metadata) -> Optional[Dict]`; `_parse_capture` calls it once and passes the result to both consumers: `_extract_inference_data(metadata, include_masks=include_masks, block=block)` and `_capture_mask_flags(block)`. `_extract_inference_data(metadata, include_masks=False, block=_UNSET)` decodes the block itself when `block` is not supplied (the five test callers), so both new keywords default to today's behaviour and each record is base64/JSON-decoded exactly once per request. `list_captures` reads two new optional query keys (`queryStringParameters` at `:155-159`): `key` (the `.jsonl` object key of one capture) and `include_masks` (`'1'`/`'true'` → true, default false). **Two request shapes on the one route, no new API Gateway resource (Req 14.3):** (i) the **page** request (no `key`) behaves as today — membership check, use-case credentials, prefix scoping (`:189-191`), `list_objects_v2` pagination (`:203-212`), up to `limit` parsed captures — and always returns the **light form**: boxes, `has_masks`, `mask_area`, `mask_truncated`, `detections_truncated`, `mask_url`, plus the new `jsonl_key` (the listed key, e.g. `device/folder/<capture_id>.jsonl`, which the viewer cannot rebuild from `capture_id` because the listing is recursive under `search_prefix`); `include_masks` is ignored on a page. A light entry is ≈ 200 B per box (`json.dumps` of the six normalised keys without `mask_rle` — `class_index`, `class_label`, `bounding_box`, `confidence`, `mask_area`, `mask_truncated`; the device emits boxes and confidences as full-precision Python floats, `rf_detr_detection_postprocessor.py:151, 201`, which `_normalize_detections` passes through, so ≈ 190–220 B rather than the 165 B a short repr would give) plus ≈ 3–5 KB of presigned URLs (three SigV4 URLs carrying the use case's session token), so the theoretical worst page (`limit` 100 × `top_k` 300 boxes on every capture) is ≈ 6.4 MB — **over** the 6 MB Lambda cap (DD4). The page bound therefore holds for `limit ≤ 50` (the viewer's and the MCP tool's maximum): the viewer's `limit: 50` page is ≈ 3.2 MB, the MCP tool's `limit_max=30` page ≈ 1.9 MB, and a page of ordinary captures is a few hundred KB; a direct API client requesting `limit` 100 over captures that all carry `top_k` instances may exceed the Lambda cap (502) and lowers `limit` (the route cap at `captures.py:159` stays 100; design review pass 5 NIT 2). `tests/test_captures_instances.py` gains a page case asserting `len(body) < 6_000_000` for 50 captures × 300 entries with realistic float reprs. (ii) The **detail** request (`key` present): after the same membership check (`check_user_access`, `:170-172`) and before the credential resolution (`get_usecase`/`get_inference_results_bucket_and_credentials`, `:175-178` — `search_prefix` depends only on `prefix` and `device_id`, so a bad key costs no STS call), `key` is validated — `str`, 1–1024 bytes (the S3 object-key maximum per AWS's key-naming guidelines, used only as an input bound; not re-verified on this host), `key.startswith(search_prefix)` (the same prefix the page would list, so a caller can read exactly the objects the page could show, and no other) and `key.endswith('.jsonl')` — else 400 `{'error': 'key must be a .jsonl object under the requested prefix'}`; the paginator is skipped and `_parse_capture(s3, bucket, key, {key}, owner_args, include_masks=include_masks)` runs once (one `get_object` with the owner assertion, no LIST), so the three presigned URLs are `None` in a detail response (`_presign_if_exists` only signs listed keys, `:374-390`; the viewer keeps the page's URLs) and the body is `{'captures': [capture], 'bucket', 'prefix': search_prefix, 'total_found': 1, 'expires_in_seconds'}` — the page's envelope, so `api.ts` needs one method. **Response bound:** the detail body carries exactly one Detection_List, ≤ 4 MiB on the device (DD4), re-serialised as ≈ 4.2 MB at most, below the 6 MB synchronous Lambda response cap and API Gateway's 10 MB (`web-answers.md` WQ-design-review-1; DD4 states why the ceiling may not be raised); a missing or unreadable object degrades inside `_parse_capture` exactly as on a page (`:253-260`: logged, entry with `detections: []`, `has_masks` false), which the viewer reports as "masks unavailable for this capture" and falls back to boxes only — never a 5xx. `limit` is ignored on a detail request. The per-capture dict adds `detections_truncated`, `has_masks` and `jsonl_key`; `mask_url` is presigned exactly as today and now exists for instance captures too. `inference_result_type` stays the device summary's verbatim value (`'Detection'` for instance captures). **Why a flag:** `GET /captures` is also the backend of the Portal MCP tool `portal_data_captures_list` (`mcp_portal/catalog.py:1845-1856`: `backend='captures'`, `handler_function='list_captures'`, `param_map` forwards `usecase_id`/`limit` only, `limit_max=30`), and the Portal MCP server has no `drop_fields` mechanism — it cuts a collection from the end until it fits `MAX_RESULT_BYTES = 200_000` (`mcp_portal/shaping.py:33, 160-180`; `_fitting` can keep zero items). With `mask_rle` in every item a page of instance captures would shrink to a few captures or to an empty list with `truncated: true`. Because neither `key` nor `include_masks` is in the tool's schema or `param_map`, the assistant always gets the light page form (`has_masks`, `mask_area`, `detections_truncated`, `mask_url`, `jsonl_key`, no RLE) and `test_mcp_catalog_arguments.argument_problems` is satisfied (rule 1 checks schema properties against the route's reads, rule 2 only *required* reads — `def argument_problems` at `test_mcp_catalog_arguments.py:71`, rule 1 `:75-85`, rule 2 `:86-100`, rules 3–4 `:101-129`; the two optional query keys read by the handler and absent from the schema violate neither). **Two requests from the Portal:** `ResultsTab.tsx` keeps its page load (`apiService.getCaptures({usecase_id, prefix, device_id, limit: 50})`, `:57-62`, unchanged — no `include_masks`) and, when the selected capture has `inference_result_type === 'Detection' && has_masks && jsonl_key`, issues the detail request `apiService.getCaptures({usecase_id, prefix, device_id, key: selected.jsonl_key, include_masks: true})` from a `useEffect` keyed on `selected?.capture_id` — with the `prefix` / `device_id` the page was **loaded** with (`response.prefix` kept beside `captures`), not the live, editable prefix input (`ResultsTab.tsx:36-42`), so an edited prefix cannot send a `key` outside the new `search_prefix` — wrapping the call in `try/catch`: a rejected promise (`apiService.request` throws `ApiError` on any non-OK status, `api.ts:1655-1661` — a 400 for a stale `jsonl_key`, a 5xx, a network failure) sets `instanceDetailState = 'unavailable'` for that `capture_id` and never touches the page-level `error` (design review pass 5 NIT 4); on success it stores `response.captures[0].detections` in a `Map<string, Detection[]>` state (`instanceDetails`) and passing `instanceDetections` / `instanceDetailState` (`'loading' | 'ready' | 'unavailable'`) to the prop-driven `ResultsViewer` — the viewer itself stays free of API calls (it is "prop-driven", `ResultsTab.tsx:31`), so its vitest cases render it with the prop and need no network stub. `api.ts getCaptures` (`:2074-2092`) gains the two optional params and appends them to the query string; the method's return type is unchanged because the detail response uses the page envelope. Rejected: porting `drop_fields` into `mcp_portal/shaping.py` (larger change, moves the Portal MCP shaping tests, and the tool's result would still be cut when boxes alone exceed the cap). This changes Requirement 11.1's default shape (RLE per selected capture on request, never on the page); recorded as correction C10. The `captures` tests gain instance records on a page (no `mask_rle` even with `include_masks=1`) and as a detail (`mask_rle` present), the 4 MiB-ceiling fixture (`len(response['body']) < 6_000_000`), the `key` validation failures (outside the prefix, not `.jsonl`, over-long → 400), a detail whose object is missing (degraded entry, 200), zero-instance, truncated and all-truncated records (Req 11.5); the five existing `_extract_inference_data` cases stay untouched, new cases cover `_capture_mask_flags` and `_decode_detections_block`, and the MCP suite asserts `portal_data_captures_list` returns no `mask_rle` for an instance fixture.

**Page cost (Req 11.1, 18.2, 18.5).** The light page trims the response (bounded above, and by DD4 for the detail), not the read: `list_captures` iterates `jsonl_keys` **sequentially** (`captures.py:214-218`), `_parse_capture` downloads each capture's `.jsonl` whole (`s3.get_object(...)['Body'].read()`, `:254`), `_load_last_metadata` decodes and JSON-parses the full body (`:280-289`), and the flags, `mask_area` and the boxes all live inside the base64 label block, so every page reads every selected record whole. The marshal embeds the Detection_List inline as base64 (`marshal_for_capture_template.py:486-491`, no size limit) and the InferenceUploader ships `**/*.jsonl` as is (`com.dda.InferenceUploader/artifacts/inference_uploader.py:130-136`), so an instance capture's `.jsonl` is ≈ 4/3 × the list: ≈ 0.4–1.4 MB for the "tens of instances" DD3 calls typical, up to ≈ 5.6 MB at the DD4 ceiling. `CapturesHandler` has the default Lambda memory (no `memorySize`, `compute-stack.ts:1409-1417`, 128 MB ⇒ a fraction of a vCPU) and a 60 s timeout behind API Gateway's fixed 29 s; `ResultsTab.tsx:57-62` requests `limit: 50` and `list_captures` allows up to 100 (`:159`, default 20). Fifty worst-case records are ≈ 280 MB of sequential GET + UTF-8 decode + JSON + base64 + JSON in 128 MB: far past 29 s; fifty typical records (≈ 20–70 MB) are a several-second page with no headroom. Mitigations, all in this spec: (1) `CapturesHandler` gets `memorySize: 1024` (`compute-stack.ts:1409-1417`; CPU share scales with memory; its `environment` stays `lambdaEnvironment`, so the exact-key oracle `grounded-sam-worker-infra.test.ts:248-318` is untouched; no IAM statement changes, so the IAM-preservation synth gate is unaffected), asserted by a case in `infrastructure/test/rfdetr-seg-infra.test.ts` (`Template.hasResourceProperties('AWS::Lambda::Function', {Handler: 'captures.handler', MemorySize: 1024})`). (2) The per-record work in `list_captures` moves to a bounded `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`: keys are processed in order-preserving slices of `limit` (`executor.map(lambda k: _parse_capture(s3, bucket, k, all_keys, owner_args), slice)` — the page path never passes `include_masks` (default `False`), so a client sending `include_masks=1` without `key` still gets the light form, as the `captures.py` paragraph states (design review pass 5 NIT 3)), non-`None` entries appended until `limit` is reached or the keys are exhausted; the per-key `try/except` (`:253-260`) stays inside `_parse_capture`, so one unreadable record still degrades to an empty-detections entry and never fails the page; the single per-request boto3 client (`captures.py:181-186`, built from the use case's credentials) is shared by the workers (boto3's documented rule, "Multithreading or multiprocessing with clients": low-level clients are thread-safe, resources and sessions are not — unverified here beyond the vendor doc; the unit test exercises the executor with a stub). (3) The light form is the only page form (above), so the MCP page and the viewer's page do no RLE serialisation and the response stays under the 6 MB Lambda cap by construction; the RLE travels in the per-capture detail request, one Detection_List per response, bounded by DD4. (4) The detail request does one `get_object` and no LIST, so selecting a capture costs one ≈ 0.4–5.6 MB read plus its JSON work, well inside 29 s even at 128 MB; with mitigation (1) it is sub-second for typical captures. Budget on paper after (1)+(2): a typical 50-capture page is ≈ 1–4 s (8 parallel GETs of ≈ 1 MB, JSON parsing ≈ 70 MB on ≈ 0.6 vCPU); the worst case (50 records at the ceiling) is bounded by the CPU-side JSON work, ≈ 10–20 s, inside 29 s but without the margin a page should have. Therefore the Requirement 18.2 run measures and records in the verification notes (Req 18.5) two figures: the light-page time for a 50-capture page of instance captures at the DD4 ceiling, and the time and `Content-Length` of one detail request for a capture at the ceiling (expected ≈ 4.2 MB, which the note compares with the 6 MB cap); **contingency:** if the measured page time exceeds 20 s, `ResultsTab.tsx:61` drops to `limit: 20` in the same task (one line; `list_captures` already pages by `limit`) and the note records the new figure; if a detail body ever measures above 5 MB, the DD4 ceiling is lowered (never the cap argued around) in the same task. Rejected: (a) moving `mask_rle` out of the inline label block into the sidecar only (would change the O1 a/O2 b device contract approved in earlier passes and make the Portal viewer read two objects per capture); (b) a listing-size budget from `Contents[].Size` (would need a paging UI the Results tab does not have, `ResultsTab.tsx:57-62`); (c) presigning the `<id>.detections.json` sidecar as a `detections_url` the browser `fetch()`es (design review pass 4, option b): a cross-origin `fetch()` — unlike the `<img src>` the viewer uses for `mask_url`/`overlay_url` (`ResultsViewer.tsx:260-262`) — needs a CORS GET rule on the inference-results bucket, and nothing in the repository configures CORS on `dda-inference-results-*` (the bucket-CORS writers are `usecases.py:558` `configure_bucket_cors` (Data Account bucket), `s3_cors.ensure_bucket_cors` (`layers/shared/python/s3_cors.py:17`, `put_bucket_cors` at `:53` / `:85`, called for use-case buckets by `data_management.py:597, 681` and `model_converter.py:349`) and `compute-stack.ts:2157` (component bucket pins); none targets `dda-inference-results-*`, and reusing `ensure_bucket_cors` would still require a `PutBucketCors` grant on the cross-account role — an IAM change through the preservation gate — and a bucket the Portal does not own; the results bucket is not created by Portal or device code — `captures.py:97-134`, `com.dda.InferenceUploader/artifacts/inference_uploader.py:36-77` only derive and assert its name), so the option would add bucket provisioning this spec has no owner for, and the sidecar is best-effort — skipped when `capture_folder`/`capture_id` is empty and swallowed on any write failure (`marshal_for_capture_template.py:241-268`, called at `:480-484`) — so the viewer would still need the inline-block fallback; (d) a page carrying every capture's RLE (design pass 3 shape): 15–50 MB at the design's own sizes, over both caps.

**`ResultsViewer.tsx` (Req 11.2, 11.3).** `Capture` gains `detections_truncated?: boolean; has_masks?: boolean; jsonl_key?: string` and `Detection` gains `mask_rle?: string; mask_area?: number; mask_truncated?: boolean`. `ResultsViewerProps` gains two optional props supplied by `ResultsTab` (§11 `captures.py`, "Two requests"): `instanceDetections?: Detection[] | null` (the detail response's entries, which carry `mask_rle`) and `instanceDetailState?: 'loading' | 'ready' | 'unavailable'`; when absent the viewer behaves exactly as today, so every existing test renders unchanged. For `inference_result_type === 'Detection' && capture.has_masks` the detection presentation draws the boxes from `capture.detections` immediately (page data) and the masks from `instanceDetections` once `instanceDetailState === 'ready'`; while `'loading'` the three-way toggle shows a Cloudscape `Spinner` beside its "masks + boxes" option and the box layer is shown alone; on `'unavailable'` (detail degraded to an empty list, §11) it shows a `StatusIndicator type="warning"` "Masks unavailable for this capture" and stays on boxes. It renders an `InstanceMaskCanvas` (a `<canvas>` positioned under the SVG box layer, sized to the rendered image, each `mask_rle` decoded with `rleToImageData` from `utils/rle.ts` (DD20) in the box's colour at 40 % alpha and drawn through an offscreen canvas scaled by `drawImage`) and the toggle becomes a three-way `SegmentedControl`: masks + boxes (default), boxes only, server overlay (disabled when `overlay_url` is null). The mask is decoded against the capture's source dimensions, which the viewer learns from the loaded `<img>` (`naturalWidth/Height`) — the RLE is at source-frame resolution (Req 10.2). Whenever `capture.detections_truncated` is set — toggle shown or not, detail loaded or not, since the page's light entries already carry `mask_truncated` — the viewer lists `#index class_label` for entries with `mask_truncated` under the toggle area ("mask omitted on device: payload limit"); for an all-truncated capture that note is the whole instance presentation (NIT 2 of design review pass 4). Detection-only (no `has_masks`) and anomaly (`mask_url` composite) presentations are untouched; semantic Seg captures are anomaly captures to the viewer (Req 11.3). Decoding is memoised per capture id (a `useMemo` on `capture.capture_id` + `instanceDetections`) so toggling does not re-decode, and `ResultsTab`'s `instanceDetails` map keeps a fetched detail for the life of the loaded page so re-selecting a capture issues no second request.

**Export (Req 11.4).** `generate_smgt_format_manifest(results)` (`inference_results_utils.py:363-384`) gains a sibling `generate_instance_manifest(results, class_map) -> List[dict]` producing one Instance_Manifest line per capture whose row carries instance `detections` (list form from the DB, DD13): `source-ref` = `os.path.basename(row["inputImageFilePath"])` — **one rule**, the precedent's (`generate_smgt_format_manifest`, `inference_results_utils.py:378`): the export zip places the images beside the manifest, so the reference is the image file name, never an `s3://` URI (the Data-contracts example shows the basename form; in a Portal-written labeling manifest the same field is the `s3://` URI the worker writes, and `_parse_instance_segmentation` accepts any non-empty string and returns it verbatim as `source_ref`, as `_parse_object_detection` does at `:632`; `parse_manifest` (`dda_manifest.py:650`) has **no caller at 43cd4ff1**, in production or in tests (`git grep parse_manifest 43cd4ff1 -- 'edge-cv-portal/**/*.py' 'src/**/*.py'` finds only its definition; the `parse_manifest_lines` hits are an unrelated `synthetic_core.py` function), so this spec's `test_dda_manifest_instance.py` round trip is its first caller; a future labeling-job seeding step would resolve `source_ref` against the job's image prefix, out of this spec's scope), `instance-segmentation = {image_size: [{width, height, depth: 3}], annotations: [{class_id, left, top, width, height, rle: mask_rle}]}` where `class_id` indexes a `class-map` built from the distinct `class_label`s in the export (sorted, stable), the box converted from `[x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max]` to `left/top/width/height` ints, and entries without `mask_rle` (truncated) skipped with a note in the export log; `instance-segmentation-metadata = {objects: [{confidence}], class-map, type: groundtruth/instance-segmentation, human-annotated: "no", creation-date, job-name: "inference-results-export"}`. Image size comes from the stored image file (`PIL`/`cv2` header read; the row has no dimensions). `src/backend/endpoints/download_file.py`'s export endpoint (`get_inference_result_data_for_retraining`, `:311-370`) gains `manifest_format: 'classification' | 'instance'` (query, default `classification`), choosing the writer; the classification manifest is byte-identical for the default (Req 11.4). **Row shape (C24):** at 43cd4ff1 the export rows carry no `detections` — `inference_result_dao.list_inference_result_data_by_capture_id_list` (`src/backend/dao/sqlite_db/inference_result_dao.py:51-56`, select `:53`) and `list_inference_result_data` (`:58-76`, selects `:64`, `:73`) project exactly `inputImageFilePath, prediction, confidence, inferenceCreationTime, humanClassification, textNote`, and the accessor dicts (`src/backend/resources/accessors/inference_result_accessor.py:108-114`, `:130-136`) unpack those six (no `detections`, no `captureId`); both entry points of `GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/results/export` (`download_file.py:346-351`; the `captureIdPath` file written by `POST /workflows/{workflow_id}/results/export`, `endpoints/inference_result.py:164-188`, read at `download_file.py:330-345`) hand those rows to the writer. So the three selects gain `InferenceResult.detections` after `textNote` and both accessor dicts gain `"detections": detections` (tuple unpack extended; `json.dump` of the `captureIdPath` file serialises the JSON-column list unchanged); `generate_instance_manifest` reads `row.get("detections") or []`, emits a line only for rows whose list carries at least one entry with `mask_rle`, and logs the count of skipped rows at INFO; `generate_smgt_format_manifest` reads named fields only (`:367-381`), so the extra key leaves the default export byte-identical. The sidecar alternative — add `captureId` to the projection and call `detections.read_detections(output_dir, capture_id)` in the writer — moves the same two projections and the same pins and adds one file read per row and a dependency on the device output layout; the projection route is taken. The Portal has no export-to-labeling route today (`captures.py` has none), so the Portal half of Req 11.4 is satisfied by the device export's zip being importable as a labeling manifest; no new API Gateway route (Req 14.3). Tests: the device suite `test/backend-test/test_inference_results_export_instance.py` covers the writer's shape and the unchanged classification export; the schema round trip through `dda_manifest._parse_instance_segmentation` lives in the Portal suite, `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/test_inference_results_export_parse.py`, which loads the real `src/backend/utils/inference_results_utils.py` the way `test_camera_shadow_sync_integration.py:80-104` loads edge packages (`sys.path.insert(0, _SRC_BACKEND)` around the import) — the device tests cannot import the Portal layer (Req 11.5).

### 12. Frontend types and pickers

Today: `DetectionArch`/`RfDetrSize` (`services/api.ts:1253-1256`), the `model_type`/`task_type` enumerations (`types/index.ts:273-320`; `transformManifest`'s `task_type` at `api.ts:3328` is the Manifest Transformer's and stays `classification | segmentation`, Req 3.5 — M-8), `RF_DETR_MODEL_TYPE_OPTIONS` (`utils/trainingSources.ts:82-89`), `RFDETR_SIZES` (`utils/detectionBaseModels.ts:33`), `INPUT_STEP`/`INPUT_BOUNDS`/`DEFAULT_SCORE_THRESHOLD` (`utils/detectorConversion.ts:38-46`), `TrainingDetail.tsx` labels (`:136-139, 635-650`), `ModelDetail.tsx` badge (`:554`), `CreateTraining.tsx` instance types (`:93-97`). O3 b, O4 b, O5 b, O13 a, O15 a; Req 12; DD7, DD20.

Types and constants (Req 12.1): `DetectionArch = 'yolo' | 'rf_detr' | 'rf_detr_seg'`; `RfDetrSegSize = 'nano' | 'small' | 'medium' | 'large' | 'xlarge' | '2xlarge'`; `OutputMode = 'instance' | 'semantic'`; the `types/index.ts` `model_type`/`task_type` enumerations gain `'instance_segmentation'` / `'InstanceSegmentation'` (and `fine_tunable.arch` gains `'rf_detr_seg'`; `transformManifest`'s `task_type` is unchanged, M-8); `detectionBaseModels.ts` gains `RFDETR_SEG_SIZES: Record<RfDetrSegSize, number>` (native resolutions), `RFDETR_SEG_INPUT_BLOCK: Record<RfDetrSegSize, 12 | 24>`, `RFDETR_SEG_RESOLUTION_BOUNDS = {min: 240, max: 1152}`, `RFDETR_SEG_QUERIES`, `validateRfDetrSegResolution(size, value)` beside the detection validator (`:339-351`), and the per-size default instance type (`ml.g4dn.xlarge` for nano/small/medium, `ml.g5.xlarge` otherwise, Req 5.3); `detectorConversion.ts` gains `INPUT_BOUNDS.rf_detr_seg = {min: 240, max: 1152}` (forced by the `Record<DetectionArch, …>` type), `DEFAULT_SCORE_THRESHOLD.rf_detr_seg = 0.5`, `DEFAULT_MASK_THRESHOLD = 0.5`, `RFDETR_SEG_NATIVE_INPUTS`, `CONVERSION_GEOMETRY.rf_detr_seg`, and its input validator skips the `% INPUT_STEP` test for `rf_detr_seg` (`:257, 297`), allowing only the native set (Req 7.3); the checkpoint-form lock helper `conversionLocksFor` and the `ConversionLocks`/`CheckpointAssessment` types are extended for `rf_detr_seg` as §8 "Seg checkpoint locks" specifies. Each constant has a vitest case mirroring the backend value (`detectionBaseModels.test.ts` pattern).

Create Training (Req 5.6, 12.2, 12.4): `RF_DETR_MODEL_TYPE_OPTIONS` gains `{value: 'instance_segmentation', label: 'Instance Segmentation (masks + boxes)'}`, filtered out when `!isRfDetrSegEnabled(config)`; selecting it shows size (six, native resolution in the label), resolution (default native; validated by block and bounds), epochs, batch, grad accum, lr, patience, score threshold, mask threshold, an Output control (`instance` | `semantic overlay`, default instance) sent as top-level `output_mode`, the Base model control filtered to records with `detection.detection_arch === 'rf_detr_seg'` (trainings) or `metadata.fine_tunable.arch === 'rf_detr_seg'` (imports) through `groupDetectionBaseModels(jobs, 'rf_detr_seg')` with `PUBLISHED_CHECKPOINTS.rf_detr_seg` (six sizes with natives), `isTrainedDetectionRecord` widened through `isDetectionModelType` (the frontend mirror of `is_trained_detection_record`, which §1 widens) and `recordDetectionArch` returning `'rf_detr_seg'` for that `detection_arch` (legacy → `'yolo'` unchanged) — the control is fed by `groupDetectionBaseModels` (`detectionBaseModels.ts:90-105`, via `apiService.listDetectionBaseModels`, `api.ts:3389-3391`, from `CreateTraining.tsx:247-252`), whose inputs `isTrainedDetectionRecord` (`:69-74`) and `recordDetectionArch` (`:77-79`) are `object_detection` / `rf_detr`-only at 43cd4ff1, so unwidened the Seg "My trained detectors" group would always be empty (C19); the Seg form is gated by `isRfDetrSeg` beside `isRfDetr` at `CreateTraining.tsx:189`; `api.ts` type sites: `DetectionRecordFields` (`:1327-1351`), the new `RfDetrSegHyperparameters` joined into `DetectionHyperparameters` (`:1304`), `createTrainingJob` body (`:3354-3373`), `convertModel` body (`:4505-4526`), `TrainingJobRecord.packaged_output_mode` (`:1471-1493`); no IoU field; the request carries `model_type: 'instance_segmentation'`, `detection_arch: 'rf_detr_seg'`, `hyperparameters: {rfdetr_seg_size, resolution, …, score_threshold, mask_threshold}`; the arch-mismatch 400 is surfaced verbatim. `LFV_MODEL_TYPE_OPTIONS` unchanged. **Form helpers (C27):** `DetectionFormParams` gains `rfdetrSegSize`, `maskThreshold`; `RFDETR_SEG_PARAM_DEFAULTS`; a `rf_detr_seg` branch in each of the seven `utils/detectionBaseModels.ts` helpers that dispatch `arch === 'rf_detr'` and otherwise take the YOLO branch — `detectionParamDefaults` (`:335-337`), `validateDetectionParams` (`:359-388`), `buildDetectionHyperparameters` (`:394-418`), `publishedBaseModelRef` (`:421-423`), `defaultDetectionInstanceType` (`:466-471`), `applyRfDetrSize` (`:477-484`, with the sibling `applyRfDetrSegSize`), `paramsFromBaseRecord` (`:490-507`) — all reached from `CreateTraining.tsx` (`:211, :228, :263, :270, :285-289, :295, :318, :573, :642`), so an unwidened Seg form would submit the YOLO hyperparameter body and `yolo11s.pt` as its base; the three inline call sites of `CreateTraining.tsx` (`:211` / `:263` size argument, `:285-289` published-base pick, the Seg size options) pass the Seg size; the three `modelType.includes('segmentation')` substring gates (`:202/:208`, `:523`, `:1001-1026`), which match `instance_segmentation` and would render the LFV "Segmentation head only" toggle for a Seg job, become `isLfvSegmentationType`.

its callers `CreateTraining.tsx:184, 200`, `trainingSources.ts:123` and `TrainingDetail.tsx` then gate correctly; no parallel helper — C16), otherwise a Seg record would show no Detection panel at all; ### 14. Infrastructure: bundles, Seg_Switch, Seg_Feature_Floor, Export_Image, LocalServer images

Today: `detectionTrainingFiles` (`edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/lib/compute-stack.ts:1491-1507`), `lambdaEnvironment` (`:713`), `WORKFLOW_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS` (`:769-776`), `streamCameraFeatureFloorEnvironment` (`:780-805`, spread at `:1302` and `:3292`), the handlers and their layers (`DeploymentsHandler :1295` `[sharedLayer, workflowCoreLayer]`, `CapturesHandler :1409`, `TrainingHandler :1548`, `PackagingHandler :1579`, `ModelImportHandler :1733`, `ModelConverterHandler :1799`, `ModelsHandler :1823` — all `[sharedLayer]`; `DdaLabelingWorker :2837` and `DdaLabelingHandler :2877` `[sharedLayer, imagingLayer]`; `WorkflowPackagingHandler :3285` `[sharedLayer, workflowCoreLayer]`; `TrainingEventsHandler :3745` `[sharedLayer]`), `context-helpers.ts` (`groundedSamWorkerEnabled :11-20`, `assistantEnabled :88-90`), `frontend/src/config.ts` (`registryEnforced :22-33`), `deploy-frontend.sh` (`:74-133`), the Export_Image (`detector-export-image/Dockerfile:64`, `build-and-push.sh:33, 83, 107-111`), `scripts/build-target-derivation.sh:30-52`, the floor coverage test (`tests/test_stream_camera_feature_floor_coverage.py:76-82, 196-225`), the CDK `test.each` (`infrastructure/test/camera-registry-stream-credentials-infra.test.ts:244-251`). D5, O11 a, O12 b; Req 14, 15; DD7, DD8.

**Bundles (Req 14.1).** `detectionTrainingFiles` gains `'detection_training/train_rfdetr_seg.py'` (both the local bundler and the Docker fallback copy the one list; the synth-time existence check covers it). `build_sourcedir_tarball` bundles per arch (`SOURCEDIR_SIBLINGS_FOR_ARCH`, DD1), so the YOLO and RF-DETR tarballs are unchanged. No IAM change: the SageMaker role already reads the use-case bucket; if the spike proves otherwise the statement is attributed in `iam_post_fix_approved_additions.json` and passes `iam_audit`.

**Layers per handler (Req 14.2).** Every handler this spec touches already has `sharedLayer` attached: `training.py` (`detection_training`), `packaging.py` (`detection_training`, `detector_conversion`), `model_converter.py` (`detection_training` — a new import in the Seg branch, DD6 — plus `detector_conversion`, `checkpoint_probe`), `model_import.py` (no new layer import), `captures.py` (no layer import added), `dda_labeling.py` and `dda_labeling_worker.py` (`dda_manifest`, already imported — the new `tight_box_from_rle` is pure Python, DD9; **no `numpy`**: these two Lambdas carry `[sharedLayer, imagingLayer]` only, `compute-stack.ts:2856, 2905`, the imaging layer is `Pillow==12.3.0` alone (`layers/imaging/requirements.txt`) and `numpy==1.26.4` exists only in `layers/video/requirements.txt`, which neither attaches; `dda-imaging-layer-empty.test.ts:295-303` pins exactly those two layers and does not move), `dda_autolabel_worker.py` (`DdaAutolabelWorker`, `compute-stack.ts:3007-3031`, `[sharedLayer, imagingLayer]`; the `_wire_modality` helper of §2 is local and its shared-layer imports `dda_llm_guidance`/`dda_llm_prelabel` already exist), `deployments.py` and `workflow_packaging.py` (floor literal, no new import), `training_events.py` (unchanged). No layer attachment changes in either topology; the design records this list so the tasks author can tick it per handler.

**Routes (Req 14.3).** No new API Gateway route. Every new operation rides an existing handler: `POST /training` (model type), `POST /models/convert` and `/models/inspect` (fields), `POST /training/{id}/package` (`output_mode`), labeling job/task/submit routes (modality), `GET /captures` (fields). The ComputeStack template size is checked after synth (`cdk synth` output size against the 1 MB limit, recorded in the verification notes).

**Seg_Switch (Req 15; DD7).** `context-helpers.ts`: `export function rfDetrSegEnabled(contextValue: unknown): boolean { return groundedSamWorkerEnabled(contextValue); }`. `compute-stack.ts`: `const rfDetrSegEnvironment = { PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED: rfDetrSegEnabled(this.node.tryGetContext('enableRfDetrSeg')) ? 'true' : 'false' };` spread into `TrainingHandler`, `ModelConverterHandler`, `PackagingHandler` only; `new cdk.CfnOutput(this, 'RfDetrSegEnabled', { value: rfDetrSegEnvironment.PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED })`. `deploy-frontend.sh` reads the output beside `AssistantApiUrl` (`:89-94`) and the Python writer (`:116-131`) sets `existing['rfDetrSegEnabled'] = (value == 'true')` when the output exists and pops the key when it is absent or `None`. `config.ts`: `rfDetrSegEnabled?: boolean` with the doc comment "Mirror of PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED; absent means ON, only an explicit false hides the RF-DETR-Seg pickers and tiles; the server stays authoritative"; `isRfDetrSegEnabled(config)`. The CDK test `infrastructure/test/rfdetr-seg-infra.test.ts`: `test.each` over the three handlers asserting `PORTAL_RFDETR_SEG_ENABLED === 'true'` with no context, `'false'` with `enableRfDetrSeg=false`, absence on `deployments.handler` / `workflow_packaging.handler` / `mcp` and on `lambdaEnvironment` consumers (one representative), and the `RfDetrSegEnabled` output value (Req 15.1, 15.3). Documentation of key/variable/default in `docs/user-guide/19-settings.md` (§15).

**Seg_Feature_Floor (Req 14.8; DD8).** `compute-stack.ts` adds, beside `streamCameraFeatureFloorEnvironment`:

```ts
// RF-DETR-Seg FEATURE floor (rfdetr-seg-support Req 14.8, design DD8): per arch, the first LocalServer
// build carrying the Seg device contract (rf_detr_instance_segmentation stage type, mask_rle in the
// Detection_List, the detections column, the mask-forwarding marshal). Read by WorkflowPackagingHandler
// and DeploymentsHandler from this one literal. Absent/empty FAILS CLOSED for every arch. Entries are
// owner-filled from tasks.md after the six criterion-5 builds exist (one change, all six keys) — see
// the enablement order in Req 14.8(d); an unverified arch is listed in
// tests/test_rfdetr_seg_feature_floor_coverage.py instead of here.
const rfDetrSegFeatureFloorEnvironment = {
  WORKFLOW_RFDETR_SEG_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS: JSON.stringify({}),   // pre-enablement: empty
};
```

spread at `:1302` and `:3292` with the stream floor. The keys, once filled, are the six `ARCH_TO_LOCAL_SERVER_COMPONENT` ids (`arm64_cpu`, `arm64_jp5`, `arm64_jp6`, `arm64_jp7`, `x86_64`, `x86_64_nvidia`; `workflow_packaging.py:397-404`). Backend: `workflow_packaging.py` parses `RFDETR_SEG_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS = _parse_min_versions_map('WORKFLOW_RFDETR_SEG_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS')` beside `:241`, `GATE_RFDETR_SEG_ARCH_UNSUPPORTED = 'RFDETR_SEG_UNSUPPORTED_ARCH'` beside `:834`, `rfdetr_seg_model_names(records) -> List[str]` (records whose `detection.detection_arch == 'rf_detr_seg'`), `rfdetr_seg_arch_gate_findings(seg_models, requested_archs)` (one finding `{code, arch, models}` per requested arch missing from the map; empty when no Seg model), `min_local_server_version_for(arch, stream_features=False, rfdetr_seg=False)` and `min_local_server_versions_map(stream_features=False, rfdetr_seg=False)` resolving the maximum of the lineage, stream and Seg floors (`PackagingError('rfdetr-seg-feature-floor/<arch>', …)` for a missing entry, `:298-309` pattern), `local_server_component_dependencies(architectures, stream_features, rfdetr_seg)`, `build_manifest(..., rfdetr_seg: bool = False)` (`:2157-2165`; threading the flag to both floor calls it makes, `:2200-2201` and `:2208-2209` — C23; at the base the signature already carries a trailing keyword-only `reference_files`, BD1), and the version-item update gains `has_rfdetr_seg_model = :hrs` (`:2931-2947`). Handler order: after `resolve_model_components` has the records (DD8: `load_model_records` first, gate, then `resolve_model_components(..., records=records)`), the 409 `RFDETR_SEG_UNSUPPORTED_ARCH` is returned with `{findings, version, architectures}` before any version item is written. `deployments.py`: `WORKFLOW_RFDETR_SEG_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS = _parse_min_versions_map(...)` beside `:270`, `rfdetr_seg_feature_floor_for(arch)` beside `:3490`, `check_local_server_compatibility(..., rfdetr_seg=False)` raising each device's minimum to `max(effective, seg_floor)` and rejecting a device whose arch has no entry (`reason: "This workflow uses an RF-DETR-Seg model, which requires a LocalServer build that supports it; no supported LocalServer version is configured for this device (architecture '<arch>')"`), the handler reading `bool(version_item.get('has_rfdetr_seg_model'))` beside `:4602` and adding `rfdetr_seg_model: True` and `rfdetr_seg_min_local_server_versions` to the 409 details beside `:4615-4623`. Workflows without a Seg model resolve exactly as today (every existing floor test passes unmodified). Coverage test `tests/test_rfdetr_seg_feature_floor_coverage.py`, modelled on `test_stream_camera_feature_floor_coverage.py`: `UNVERIFIED_RFDETR_SEG_ARCHES = {}` initially (O11 a rebuilds all six), regex-extracts the literal from `compute-stack.ts` (`extract_env_json_map`, `:85-120`), skips on absent/empty, and once non-empty asserts the key set equals `ARCH_TO_LOCAL_SERVER_COMPONENT` minus the unverified set, well-formed `N.N.N` values, the gate wired (`rfdetr_seg_arch_gate_findings` rejects an arch outside the map), and non-Seg workflows unchanged. CDK test (`rfdetr-seg-infra.test.ts`, the `camera-registry-stream-credentials-infra.test.ts:244-251` pattern): `test.each(['workflow_packaging.handler', 'deployments.handler'])` carries `WORKFLOW_RFDETR_SEG_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS`, the parsed value equals the literal (empty object pre-enablement), and no other Lambda carries it — this is the CDK test Req 17.6 refers to (requirements-review pass 5 NIT 2; see "Requirements corrections", C2). `WORKFLOW_MIN_LOCAL_SERVER_VERSIONS` and the stream literal are untouched (Req 14.8f, 17.3).

**Enablement order and the coverage test (NIT 1).** Because the coverage test accepts only an absent/empty literal before full coverage, the six entries are entered in **one change** after all six criterion-5 builds exist; an arch whose build is late is listed in `UNVERIFIED_RFDETR_SEG_ARCHES` with its reason until it lands. For JP7 and JP6 the value entered in that change is the **candidate** (the first criterion-5 build), the Portal is redeployed, the Req 18 run follows, and a fixed build raises the entry (second owner-filled value) — see "Requirements corrections", C1, for the one-sentence requirements fix tasks.md carries as task 0.x.

**Export_Image (Req 7.8, 14.4).** `detector-export-image/Dockerfile:64` and `build-and-push.sh:83` copy `train_rfdetr_seg.py` beside `train_rfdetr.py` and `_common.py` (a code-only layer; pins unchanged); the smoke is extended to `python -c "import export_checkpoint, train_rfdetr, train_rfdetr_seg"` — `export_checkpoint.py:843` imports the trainer lazily inside `convert_rfdetr`, so the existing smoke never imports either trainer module (tasks review pass 1, finding 3; C14). The rebuild is mandatory for the code change, not optional: the job's code is baked into the image (`Dockerfile:64` `COPY`, `:68` `ENTRYPOINT`; `detector_conversion.py:493-494, 531` `EnableNetworkIsolation=True`, no `sagemaker_submit_directory`), so the edited `export_checkpoint.py` and the new `train_rfdetr_seg.py` reach a job only through a new image (Requirement 14.4 as corrected by C14). The image is rebuilt once for the code change and its digest recorded on every Conversion_Record as today (`-c detectorExportImage=<repo>@sha256:<digest>`); if the spike proves a pin change, `requirements.in` → `--lock` → `requirements.lock` precede it.

**LocalServer images (Req 14.5, 14.6; O11 a).** No Dockerfile edits are expected: the device changes are Python modules and templates already copied by every Dockerfile (`src/backend/Dockerfile:53-64` and the JP variants), plus `numpy`/`cv2` already pinned (`src/backend/requirements.txt:7, 23`); the Alembic migration ships with the app and runs at start as today. All six components are rebuilt through `build-custom.sh` per `scripts/build-target-derivation.sh:30-52` (`.amd64` and `.arm64` from `Dockerfile`, `.amd64Nvidia`, `.arm64JP5`, `.arm64JP6`, `.arm64JP7`), one at a time (`.kiro/steering/builds.md`). **The D5 component is replaced, not re-packaged** (Req 14.6, second alternative). Its record is an imported ONNX record (`model_type = 'segmentation'`, `detection_arch = 'rf_detr'`, `source = 'imported'`), which `is_onnx_import` routes to `package_onnx_component` (`packaging.py:201-221`, `:224-336`); that function downloads the imported package and re-ships the `export_artifacts/manifest.json` written by `generate_dda_package` **at convert time** (`:259-266`), merging only the `dataset` block (`:287-298`) — it never regenerates the manifest, and §8 keeps it unchanged (Req 8.6). A "re-package" would therefore republish the old manifest (stage-level `detection`, no `output_mode`, no `output_names`) and the record would stay ungated by DD8 (it keeps `detection_arch = 'rf_detr'`). So the same Seg-Nano ONNX is **re-imported** through the hardened path: `POST /models/convert` with `model_type = 'instance_segmentation'`, `output_mode = 'semantic'`, the 90 COCO class names (Req 8.2 requires names; `num_classes = 91 − 1`), `score_threshold`/`mask_threshold` 0.5, producing a new record (`detection_arch = 'rf_detr_seg'`, gated by DD8) and a new component whose name the owner fills in `tasks.md` (candidate: `model-rf-detr-seg-nano-semantic-jetson-xavier-jp6`); the old record and component are left in place (D5 permits either; nothing deletes a published component). `test/on-hardware/harness/README.md:76, 327` and the harness stage that expects the old name are updated to the new one in the same change, and the Req 18 JP6 run deploys the new component.

**`CapturesHandler` memory (Req 11.1; §11 "Page cost").** `compute-stack.ts:1409-1417` gains `memorySize: 1024` (today the function declares none, so it runs at the 128 MB default; the handler downloads and decodes every selected `.jsonl` whole). This is a property change on an existing `AWS::Lambda::Function`: no new CFN resource (the ApiGatewayStack count and the ComputeStack template size are unaffected beyond a few bytes), no IAM statement (`capturesRole`, `:1408`, is untouched, so the IAM-preservation synth gate is unaffected; `captures.py` itself is not in `iam_out_of_scope_baseline.json`, and the handler's new code adds no AWS call beyond the `get_object` reads it already makes), `environment` stays `lambdaEnvironment` (exact-key oracle untouched). No existing CDK test pins `CapturesHandler`'s properties (`grep -rn 'captures.handler' edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/test` finds nothing at 43cd4ff1; the function-configuration oracle in `dda-imaging-layer-empty.test.ts:303-330` iterates `DDA_LABELING_FUNCTION_PREFIXES` only), so nothing moves; the new assertion lives in `rfdetr-seg-infra.test.ts`.

**Topologies (Req 14.7).** The CDK changes are in `EdgeCVPortalComputeStack` only (environment literals, a `CfnOutput`, the `CapturesHandler` `memorySize`); they deploy identically in the single-account and use-case-account topologies (`CapturesHandler` is declared once, in `compute-stack.ts`; `usecase-account-stack.ts` declares no Lambda, and the use-case account is reached from this handler through the use case's credentials, `captures.py:177-184`, which do not change), and no cross-account IAM statement changes. The verification notes record both topologies' `cdk diff` (environment and the one `MemorySize` property) after moving `cdk.out` aside for the drift guards.

### 15. Documentation

O14 a; Req 16.
